C_{1}(M,E_{0},T,\varepsilon):=\big(4C_{0}(M,E_{0})\big)^{\frac{2}{\alpha}}\left(\frac{1+T}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{\gamma}{\alpha(\gamma-1)}}
.
Then we use (
3.12
) and (
3.68
) to conclude that

(3.69)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{t}u(s,b(s))\,{\rm d}s\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{b}{4},

provided
b\geq C_{1}(M,E_{0},T,\varepsilon)
. Then it follows from (
3.67
) and (
3.69
) that

(3.70)

b(t)\geq\frac{3b}{4}.

Thus, we have closed our
a priori
assumption (
3.66
).
Then, using (
3.70
) and the continuity arguments, we conclude (
3.65
).
∎

Lemma 3.5

(Higher integrability on the density)

Let
(\rho,u)
be the smooth solution of (
3.1
)–(
3.6
).
Then, under the assumption of Lemma
3.1
,

(3.71)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\rho^{\gamma+1}(t,r)\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\leq C(K,M,E_{0},T)

for any
K\Subset(a,b(t))
and any
t\in[0,T]
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into five steps.

1. For given
K\Subset(a,b(t))
for any
t\in[0,T]
,
there exist
d
and
D
such that
K\Subset(d,D)\Subset[a,b(t)]
.
Let
w(r)
be a smooth compact support function with
\text{supp}\,w\subseteq(d,D)
and
w(r)=1
for
r\in K
.
Multiplying
\eqref{2.1}_{2}
by
w(r)
, we have

(3.72)

\displaystyle(\rho uw)_{t}+\big((\rho u^{2}+p(\rho))w\big)_{r}+\frac{n-1}{r}\rho u^{2}w

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\big(\rho(u_{r}+\frac{n-1}{r}u)w\big)_{r}-\varepsilon\frac{n-1}{r}u\rho_{r}w

\displaystyle\quad+\big(\rho u^{2}+p(\rho)-\varepsilon\rho(u_{r}+\frac{n-1}{r}u)\big)w_{r}-\frac{\kappa\rho w}{r^{n-1}}\int_{a}^{r}\rho\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z.

Integrating (
3.72
) over
[d,r)
to obtain

(3.73)

\displaystyle p(\rho)w

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\rho\big(u_{r}+\frac{n-1}{r}u\big)w-\varepsilon\int_{d}^{r}\frac{n-1}{z}u\rho_{z}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\quad-\Big(\big(\int_{d}^{r}\rho uw\,{\rm d}z\big)_{t}+\rho u^{2}w\Big)-\int_{d}^{r}\frac{n-1}{z}\rho u^{2}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{d}^{r}\big(\rho u^{2}+p(\rho)-\varepsilon\rho(u_{z}+\frac{n-1}{z}u)\big)w_{z}\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\int_{d}^{r}\big(\int_{a}^{z}\rho\,y^{n-1}dy\big)\frac{\rho w}{z^{n-1}}{\rm d}z.

Multiplying (
3.73
) by
\rho w
, we have

(3.74)

\displaystyle\rho\,p(\rho)w^{2}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\rho^{2}\big(u_{r}+\frac{n-1}{r}u\big)w^{2}-\varepsilon\rho w\int_{d}^{r}\frac{n-1}{z}u\rho_{z}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\quad-\Big(\rho w\big(\int_{d}^{r}\rho uw\,{\rm d}z\big)_{t}+\rho^{2}u^{2}w^{2}\Big)-\rho w\int_{d}^{r}\frac{n-1}{z}\rho u^{2}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\rho w\int_{d}^{r}\big(\rho u^{2}+p(\rho)-\varepsilon\rho(u_{z}+\frac{n-1}{z}u)\big)w_{z}\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\rho w\int_{d}^{r}\big(\int_{a}^{z}\rho\,y^{n-1}dy\big)\frac{\rho w}{z^{n-1}}{\rm d}z.

Notice that

\displaystyle\rho w\Big(\int_{d}^{r}\rho uw\,{\rm d}z\Big)_{t}+\rho^{2}u^{2}w^{2}

\displaystyle=\Big(\rho w\int_{d}^{r}\rho uw\,{\rm d}z\Big)_{t}+\Big(\rho uw\int_{d}^{r}\rho uw\,{\rm d}z\Big)_{r}

\displaystyle\quad-\rho uw_{r}\int_{d}^{r}\rho uw\,{\rm d}z+\frac{n-1}{r}\rho uw\int_{d}^{r}\rho uw\,{\rm d}z.

Then it following from
(
3.74
) that

(3.75)

\displaystyle\rho p(\rho)w^{2}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\rho^{2}\big(u_{r}+\frac{n-1}{r}u\big)w^{2}-\varepsilon\rho w\int_{d}^{r}\frac{n-1}{z}u\rho_{z}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\quad-\Big(\rho w\int_{d}^{r}\rho uw\,{\rm d}z\Big)_{t}-\Big(\rho uw\int_{d}^{r}\rho uw\,{\rm d}z\Big)_{r}

\displaystyle\quad+\rho uw_{r}\int_{d}^{r}\rho uw\,{\rm d}z-\frac{n-1}{r}\rho uw\int_{d}^{r}\rho uw\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\rho w\int_{d}^{r}\Big(\rho u^{2}+p(\rho)-\varepsilon\rho(u_{z}+\frac{n-1}{z}u)\Big)w_{z}\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\quad-\rho w\int_{d}^{r}\frac{n-1}{z}\rho u^{2}w\,{\rm d}z-\kappa\rho w\int_{d}^{r}\big(\int_{a}^{z}\rho\,y^{n-1}dy\big)\frac{\rho w}{z^{n-1}}\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle:=\sum_{i=1}^{9}I_{i}.

2. To estimate
I_{i},i=1,\cdots,9
, in (
3.75
), we first notice that

(3.76)

\displaystyle\int_{d}^{D}(\rho+\rho|u|)\,{\rm d}r\leq\frac{C}{d^{n-1}}\int_{d}^{D}(\rho+\rho u^{2})\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r\leq C(d,M,E_{0}).

Then it follows from (
3.76
) that

(3.77)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}I_{3}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\int_{d}^{D}(\rho w)(T,r)\,\Big(\int_{d}^{r}(\rho uw)(T,z)\,{\rm d}z\Big)\,{\rm d}r\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\left|\int_{d}^{D}(\rho w)(0,r)\,\Big(\int_{d}^{r}(\rho uw)(0,z)\,{\rm d}z\Big)\,{\rm d}r\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\,\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\{\int_{d}^{D}\rho(t,r)\,{\rm d}r\,\int_{d}^{D}(\rho|u|)(t,z)\,{\rm d}z\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C(d,M,E_{0},T),

(3.78)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\big(I_{5}+I_{6}+I_{8}\big)\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\right|

\displaystyle\leq C(d)\int_{0}^{T}\left(\int_{d}^{D}(\rho+\rho|u|^{2})(t,r)\,{\rm d}r\right)\left(\int_{d}^{r}(\rho+\rho|u|^{2})(t,z)\,{\rm d}z\right)\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,M,E_{0},T),

and

(3.79)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}I_{9}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\right|

\displaystyle\leq C(d)\int_{0}^{T}\left(\int_{d}^{D}\rho(t,r)\,{\rm d}r\right)\left(\int_{d}^{r}\rho(t,z)\,{\rm d}z\right)\left(\int_{d}^{z}\rho(t,y)\,y^{n-1}\,{\rm d}y\right){\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d)\int_{0}^{T}\left(\int_{d}^{D}\rho(t,r)\,r^{n-1}\,{\rm d}r\right)^{3}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,M,E_{0},T).

Since
\mbox{supp}\,w\subseteq(d,D)
, it is clear that

(3.80)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}I_{4}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\right|=\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\Big(\rho uw\int_{d}^{r}\rho uw\,{\rm d}z\Big)_{r}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\right|=0.

3. Next, we estimate
I_{7}
. Noting that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\rho w\int_{d}^{r}\big(\rho u^{2}+p(\rho)\big)w_{z}\,{\rm d}z{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\Big|\leq C(d,M,E_{0},T),

\displaystyle\varepsilon\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\rho w\int_{d}^{r}\rho(u_{z}+\frac{n-1}{z}u)w_{z}\,{\rm d}z{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(d,M,E_{0})\Big(\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\big(z^{n-1}\rho|u_{z}|^{2}+z^{n-1}\rho u^{2}\big)\,{\rm d}z{\rm d}t\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(d,M,E_{0},T),

we have

(3.81)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}I_{7}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\Big|\leq C(d,M,E_{0},T).

4. For
I_{2}
, integrating by parts, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\int^{r}_{d}\frac{n-1}{z}u\rho_{z}w\,{\rm d}z\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n-1}{r}|(\rho uw)(t,r)|+C\Big|\int_{d}^{r}\frac{1}{z}\Big(\rho u_{z}w+\rho uw_{z}-\frac{1}{z}\rho uw\Big)(t,z){\rm d}z\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n-1}{r}|(\rho uw)(t,r)|+C(d)\int_{d}^{D}\rho u_{r}^{2}\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r+C(d,M,E_{0},T),

which yields that

(3.82)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}I_{2}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\varepsilon\rho w\int_{d}^{r}\frac{n-1}{z}u\rho_{z}w\,{\rm d}z{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C(d,M,E_{0},T)\Big(1+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\varepsilon\rho u_{r}^{2}\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\Big)+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\rho^{3}w^{2}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,M,E_{0},T)+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\rho^{3}w^{2}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t.

For
I_{1}
, notice that

(3.83)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}I_{1}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\rho^{2}\big|u_{r}+\frac{n-1}{r}u\big|w^{2}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\rho\Big(\rho^{2}+C\big(u_{r}+\frac{n-1}{r}u\big)^{2}\Big)w^{2}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,M,E_{0},T)+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\rho^{3}w^{2}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t.

To close the estimates for
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
, we need to bound the last term of both (
3.82
)-RHS and (
3.83
)-RHS.
There are three cases:

Case
1:
\gamma\in(1,2]
. Notice that

(3.84)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\rho^{3}w^{2}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\big(\int_{d}^{D}\rho^{\gamma}{\rm d}r\big)\sup_{r\in[d,D]}\big(\rho^{3-\gamma}w^{2}\big)\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,M,E_{0})\int_{0}^{T}\varepsilon\sup_{r\in[d,D]}\big(\rho^{3-\gamma}w^{2}\big)\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,M,E_{0})\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\varepsilon\big|\big(\rho^{3-\gamma}w^{2}\big)_{r}(t,r)\big|\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}(d,M,E_{0})\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\varepsilon\big(\rho^{2-\gamma}|\rho_{r}|w^{2}+\rho^{3-\gamma}w|w_{r}|\big)\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t.

A direct calculation shows that

(3.85)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\varepsilon\rho^{2-\gamma}|\rho_{r}|w^{2}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\varepsilon\rho^{\gamma-2}\rho_{r}^{2}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\rho^{3(2-\gamma)}w^{2}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,M,E_{0},T)+\frac{1}{4C_{2}(d,M,E_{0})}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\varepsilon\rho^{3}w^{2}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t,

and

(3.86)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\varepsilon\rho^{3-\gamma}w|w_{r}|\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\varepsilon\sup_{r\in[d,D]}(\rho w)(t,r)\int_{d}^{D}\rho^{2-\gamma}|w_{r}|\,{\rm d}r\Big){\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,M,E_{0})\int_{0}^{T}\varepsilon\sup_{r\in[d,D]}(\rho w)(t,r)\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,M,E_{0})\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\varepsilon\big(|\rho_{r}|w+\rho|w_{r}|\big)\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,M,E_{0})\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\varepsilon\rho^{\gamma-2}\rho_{r}^{2}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\big(\rho+\rho^{2-\gamma}w\big)\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(d,M,E_{0},T).

Combining (
3.84
)–(
3.86
), we have

(3.87)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\rho^{3}w^{2}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\leq C(d,M,E_{0},T)\qquad\mbox{for $\gamma\in(1,2]$}.

Case
2:
\gamma\in[2,3]
. We have

(3.88)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\rho^{3}w^{2}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\sup_{r\in[d,D]}(\rho^{2}w)(t,r)\int_{d}^{D}\rho w\,{\rm d}r\Big){\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,M,E_{0})\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\varepsilon\big(\rho|\rho_{r}|w+\rho^{2}|w_{r}|\big)\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,M,E_{0})\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\big(\varepsilon^{2}\rho^{\gamma-2}|\rho_{r}|^{2}w+\rho^{2}|w_{r}|+\rho^{4-\gamma}w\big)\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,M,E_{0},T).

Case
3:
\gamma\in(3,\infty)
. It is direct to see that

(3.89)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\rho^{3}w^{2}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}(\rho+\rho^{\gamma})\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\leq C(d,M,E_{0},T).

Now substituting (
3.87
)–(
3.89
) into (
3.82
)–(
3.83
) yields that

(3.90)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}(I_{1}+I_{2})\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\Big|\leq C(d,M,E_{0},T).

5. Integrating (
3.75
) over
[0,T]\times[d,D]
and
then using (
3.77
)–(
3.81
) and (
3.90
),
we conclude (
3.71
).
∎

To use the compensated compactness framework in
[
8
]
,
we still need to obtain the higher integrability on the velocity.
For this, we require to exploit several important properties of some special entropy pairs.

First, taking
\psi(s)=\frac{1}{2}{s}{|s|}
in (
2.11
), then the corresponding entropy and entropy flux are represented as

(3.91)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\eta^{\#}(\rho,\rho u)=\frac{1}{2}\rho\int_{-1}^{1}(u+\rho^{\theta}s)|u+\rho^{\theta}s|[1-s^{2}]_{+}^{\mathfrak{b}}{\rm d}s,\\[8.53581pt]
\displaystyle q^{\#}(\rho,\rho u)=\frac{1}{2}\rho\int_{-1}^{1}(u+\theta\rho^{\theta}s)(u+\rho^{\theta}s)|u+\rho^{\theta}s|[1-s^{2}]_{+}^{\mathfrak{b}}{\rm d}s.\end{cases}

A direct calculation shows that

(3.92)

\displaystyle|\eta^{\#}(\rho,\rho u)|\leq C_{\gamma}\big(\rho|u|^{2}+\rho^{\gamma}\big),\qquad\,\,q^{\#}(\rho,\rho u)\geq C_{\gamma}^{-1}\big(\rho|u|^{3}+\rho^{\gamma+\theta}\big),

where and whereafter
C_{\gamma}>0
is a universal constant depending only on
\gamma>1
.
We regard
\eta^{\#}
as a function of
(\rho,m)
to obtain

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\eta^{\#}_{\rho}=\int_{-1}^{1}\big(-\frac{1}{2}u+(\theta+\frac{1}{2})\rho^{\theta}s\big)\,|u+\rho^{\theta}s|[1-s^{2}]_{+}^{\mathfrak{b}}{\rm d}s,\\[8.53581pt]
\displaystyle\eta^{\#}_{m}=\int_{-1}^{1}|u+\rho^{\theta}s|[1-s^{2}]_{+}^{\mathfrak{b}}{\rm d}s.\end{cases}

It is direct to check that

(3.93)

\displaystyle|\eta^{\#}_{m}|\leq C_{\gamma}\big(|u|+\rho^{\theta}\big),\qquad\,\,|\eta^{\#}_{\rho}|\leq C_{\gamma}\big(|u|^{2}+\rho^{2\theta}\big).

From
[
8
,
9
]
, we know that

(3.94)

\displaystyle\rho u\partial_{\rho}\eta^{\#}+\rho u^{2}\partial_{m}\eta^{\#}-q^{\#}

\displaystyle=\frac{\theta}{2}\rho^{1+\theta}\int_{-1}^{1}(u-\rho^{\theta}s)s|u+\rho^{\theta}s|\,[1-s^{2}]_{+}^{\mathfrak{b}}{\rm d}s\leq 0.

The following lemma is crucial to control the trace estimates for the higher integrability on the velocity.
In fact, we have the boundary parts
(u\eta^{\#})(t,b(t))
and
q^{\#}(t,b(t))
,
and it is impossible to have the uniform trace bound (independent of
\varepsilon
) for each of them.
Our key point is to identify the cancelation between these two boundary parts.

Lemma 3.6

For the entropy pair defined in (
3.91
),

(3.95)

\displaystyle|q^{\#}-u\eta^{\#}|\leq C_{\gamma}\big(\rho^{\gamma}|u|+\rho^{\gamma+\theta}\big).

Proof.

It follows from (
3.91
) that

(3.96)

\displaystyle q^{\#}-u\eta^{\#}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\theta\rho^{1+2\theta}\int_{-1}^{1}s^{2}|u+\rho^{\theta}|[1-s^{2}]_{+}^{\mathfrak{b}}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\theta\rho^{1+\theta}u\int_{-1}^{1}s|u+\rho^{\theta}s|[1-s^{2}]_{+}^{\mathfrak{b}}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle:=I_{1}+I_{2}.

A direct calculation shows that

(3.97)

|I_{1}|\leq C_{\gamma}\big(\rho^{\gamma}|u|+\rho^{\gamma+\theta}\big).

For
I_{2}
, we note that
I_{2}=0
if
u=0
. Thus, it suffices to consider
u\neq 0
. We divide the proof into three cases.

Case
1. If
u>0
and
u+\rho^{\theta}s\geq 0
for all
s\in[-1,1]
, then it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}s|u+\rho^{\theta}s|[1-s^{2}]_{+}^{\mathfrak{b}}\,{\rm d}s

\displaystyle=u\int_{-1}^{1}s[1-s^{2}]_{+}^{\mathfrak{b}}\,{\rm d}s+\rho^{\theta}\int_{-1}^{1}s^{2}[1-s^{2}]_{+}^{\mathfrak{b}}\,{\rm d}s

\displaystyle=\rho^{\theta}\int_{-1}^{1}s^{2}[1-s^{2}]_{+}^{\mathfrak{b}}\,{\rm d}s,

which
yields that

(3.98)

|I_{2}|\leq C_{\gamma}\rho^{1+2\theta}|u|=C_{\gamma}\rho^{\gamma}|u|.

Case
2.
If
u>0
and
u+\rho^{\theta}s_{0}=0
for some
s_{0}\in[-1,1]
, then

s_{0}=-\frac{u}{\rho^{\theta}}\in[-1,1]
so that

|u|\leq\rho^{\theta},

which yields that

(3.99)

\displaystyle|I_{2}|\leq C_{\gamma}\rho^{1+\theta}|u|\big(|u|+\rho^{\theta}\big)\leq C_{\gamma}\rho^{1+3\theta}=C_{\gamma}\rho^{\gamma+\theta}.

Case
3. If
u<0
, by similar arguments as in Cases 1–2,

|I_{2}|\leq C_{\gamma}\big(\rho^{\gamma}|u|+\rho^{\gamma+\theta}\big),

which, together with (
3.96
)–(
3.99
) yields (
3.95
).
∎

Lemma 3.7

(Higher integrability on the velocity)

Let
(\rho,u)
be the smooth solution of (
3.1
)–(
3.3
).
Then, under the assumption of Lemma
3.1
,

(3.100)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\big(\rho|u|^{3}+\rho^{\gamma+\theta}\big)(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\leq C(d,D,M,E_{0},T)

for any
(d,D)\Subset[a,b(t)]
.

Proof.

Multiplying
\eqref{2.1}_{1}
by
r^{n-1}\eta^{\#}_{\rho}
and
\eqref{2.1}_{2}
by
r^{n-1}\eta^{\#}_{m}
,
we have

(3.101)

\displaystyle(\eta^{\#}r^{n-1})_{t}+(q^{\#}r^{n-1})_{r}+(n-1)\big(-q^{\#}+\rho u\eta^{\#}_{\rho}+\rho u^{2}\eta^{\#}_{m}\big)r^{n-2}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\eta^{\#}_{m}\,\Big((\rho u_{r})_{r}+(n-1)\rho\Big(\frac{u}{r}\Big)_{r}\Big)r^{n-1}-\kappa\eta^{\#}_{m}\,\rho\int_{a}^{r}\rho\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z.

Using (
3.3
), a direct calculation shows that

(3.102)

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\int_{r}^{b(t)}\eta^{\#}\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z

\displaystyle=\eta^{\#}(t,b(t))b(t)^{n-1}b^{\prime}(t)+\int_{r}^{b(t)}\eta^{\#}_{t}(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z

\displaystyle=(u\eta^{\#})(t,b(t))b(t)^{n-1}+\int_{r}^{b(t)}\eta^{\#}_{t}(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z.

Integrating (
3.101
) over
[r,b(t))
, then using (
3.94
) and (
3.102
), we have

(3.103)

\displaystyle q^{\#}(t,r)r^{n-1}

\displaystyle\leq-\varepsilon\int_{r}^{b(t)}\eta^{\#}_{m}(t,z)(\rho u_{z})_{z}\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z-(n-1)\varepsilon\int_{r}^{b(t)}\eta^{\#}_{m}(t,z)\rho\big(\frac{u}{z}\big)_{z}\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\quad\,+\Big(\int_{r}^{b(t)}\eta^{\#}(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\Big)_{t}+\big(q^{\#}-u\eta^{\#}\big)(t,b(t))b(t)^{n-1}

\displaystyle\quad\,+\kappa\int_{r}^{b(t)}\big(\int_{a}^{y}\rho\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\big)\rho\,\eta^{\#}_{m}\,{\rm d}y.

We now bound each term of (
3.103
)-RHS. First,
for the term involving the trace estimates in (
3.103
), it follows from (
3.47
) and Lemmas
3.1
,
3.3
, and
3.6

that

(3.104)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\big|(q^{\#}-u\eta^{\#})(t,b(t))\big|b(t)^{n-1}\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\big(\rho^{\gamma+\theta}(t,b(t))+(\rho^{\gamma}|u|)(t,b(t))\big)b(t)^{n-1}\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\varepsilon(\rho|u|^{2})(t,b(t))b(t)^{n-2}\,{\rm d}t\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\rho^{2\gamma-1}(t,b(t))b(t)^{n}\,{\rm d}t\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\,\quad+C(M,E_{0},T)\int_{0}^{T}\rho^{{\frac{\gamma}{n}+\theta}}(t,b(t))\,{\rm d}t\leq C(M,E_{0},T).

Observe from (
3.104
) that the free boundary approximation is
ideal for the existence of solutions of CEPEs with finite mass.

To estimate the first term of (
3.103
)-RHS, we integrate by parts to obtain

(3.105)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\int_{r}^{b(t)}\eta^{\#}_{m}(\rho u_{z})_{z}\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\Big(\eta^{\#}_{m}(t,b(t))\,(\rho u_{r})(t,b(t))b(t)^{n-1}-\eta^{\#}_{m}(t,r)(\rho u_{r})(t,r)r^{n-1}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\,-(n-1)\varepsilon\int_{r}^{b(t)}\eta^{\#}_{m}\,\rho u_{z}\,z^{n-2}{\rm d}z-\varepsilon\int_{r}^{b(t)}\rho u_{z}(\eta^{\#}_{mu}u_{z}+\eta^{\#}_{m\rho}\rho_{z})\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z,

where we have regarded
\eta^{\#}_{m}
as a function of
(\rho,u)
. Using (
3.4
) and
(
3.93
)
, we have

(3.106)

\displaystyle\big|\varepsilon\eta^{\#}_{m}(t,b(t))\,(\rho u_{r})(t,b(t))b(t)^{n-1}\big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\eta^{\#}_{m}(t,b(t))\,\Big(\varepsilon\rho\big(u_{r}+\frac{n-1}{r}u\big)(t,b(t))-(n-1)\varepsilon b(t)^{-1}\,(\rho u)(t,b(t))\Big)b(t)^{n-1}\Big|

\displaystyle=\left|a_{0}\eta^{\#}_{m}(t,b(t))\,\rho^{\gamma}(t,b(t))b(t)^{n-1}-(n-1)\varepsilon\eta^{\#}_{m}(t,b(t))\,(\rho u)(t,b(t))b(t)^{n-2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\big\{(\rho^{\gamma}|u|)(t,b(t))+\rho^{\gamma+\theta}(t,b(t))\big\}b(t)^{n-1}

\displaystyle\quad\,+C\varepsilon\big\{(\rho|u|^{2})(t,b(t))+(\rho^{1+\theta}|u|)(t,b(t))\big\}b(t)^{n-2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big\{(\rho^{\gamma}|u|)(t,b(t))+\rho^{\gamma+\theta}(t,b(t))\big\}b(t)^{n-1}

\displaystyle\quad\,+C\varepsilon\big\{(\rho|u|^{2})(t,b(t))+\rho^{\gamma}(t,b(t))\big\}b(t)^{n-2}.

Thus, using (
3.106
), by similar arguments as in (
3.104
), we have

(3.107)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\big|\varepsilon b^{n-1}(t)\eta^{\#}_{m}(t,b(t))(\rho u_{r})(t,b(t))\big|\,{\rm d}t\leq C(M,E_{0},T).

A direct calculation shows that

(3.108)

\displaystyle|\eta^{\#}_{mu}|+\rho^{1-\theta}|\eta^{\#}_{m\rho}|\leq C.

Integrating (
3.105
) over
[0,T]\times[d,D]
and then using (
3.93
) and (
3.107
)–(
3.108
) lead to

(3.109)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\Big|\varepsilon\int_{r}^{b(t)}\eta^{\#}_{m}\,(\rho u_{z})_{z}\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\Big|{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(D,M,E_{0},T)+C\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\rho|u_{r}|\big(|u|+\rho^{\theta}\big)\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad\,+C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\int_{r}^{b(t)}\varepsilon\rho|u_{z}|\big(|u|+\rho^{\theta}\big)\,z^{n-2}{\rm d}z{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad\,+C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\int_{r}^{b(t)}\varepsilon\rho|u_{z}|\big(|u_{z}|+\rho^{\theta-1}|\rho_{z}|\big)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(D,M,E_{0},T)+C(d,D)\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{b(t)}\varepsilon\big(\rho|u_{z}|^{2}+\rho^{\gamma-2}|\rho_{z}|^{2}\big)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad\,+C(d,D)\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{b(t)}\varepsilon\big(\rho|u|^{2}+\rho^{\gamma}\big)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,D,M,E_{0},T),

where we have used Lemmas
3.1
–
3.5
.

For the second term of (
3.103
)-RHS, we have

(3.110)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\Big|\int_{r}^{b(t)}\varepsilon\eta^{\#}_{m}(t,z)\,\rho(\frac{u}{z})_{z}\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\Big|{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(D)\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{b(t)}\varepsilon\big(\rho|u|+\rho^{1+\theta}\big)\big(\frac{|u_{z}|}{z}+\frac{|u|}{z^{2}}\big)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle\leq C(d,D)\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{b(t)}\varepsilon\big(\rho|u|^{2}+\rho|u_{z}|^{2}+\rho^{\gamma}\big)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,D,M,E_{0},T).

For the third term of (
3.103
)-RHS,

(3.111)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\Big(\int_{r}^{b(t)}\eta^{\#}(\rho,\rho u)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\Big)_{t}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\int_{d}^{D}\int_{r}^{b(T)}\eta^{\#}(\rho,\rho u)(T,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z{\rm d}r\Big|

\displaystyle\quad\,+\Big|\int_{d}^{D}\int_{r}^{b}\eta^{\#}(\rho_{0},\rho_{0}u_{0})\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z{\rm d}r\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C(D,M,E_{0}).

For the last term of (
3.103
)-RHS, it follows from (
3.92
) that

(3.112)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\Big|\kappa\int_{r}^{b(t)}\eta^{\#}_{m}\,\rho\big(\int_{a}^{y}\rho\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\big)dy\Big|{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(D,M)\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{b(t)}\big(\rho|u|+\rho^{1+\theta}\big)\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(D,M)\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{b(t)}\big(\rho|u|^{2}+\rho+\rho^{\gamma}\big)\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\leq C(d,D,M,E_{0}).

Integrating (
3.103
) over
[0,T]\times[d,D]
, then using (
3.92
), (
3.104
), and (
3.109
)–(
3.112
),
we conclude that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\big(\rho|u|^{3}+\rho^{\gamma+\theta}\big)\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}q^{\#}(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\leq C(d,D,M,E_{0},T).

∎

4
Existence of Global Finite-Energy Solutions

In this section, for fixed
\varepsilon>0
, we take limit
b\rightarrow\infty
to obtain
global weak solutions of CNSPEs with some uniform bounds,
which are essential for applying the compensated compactness framework in §5 below.
We often denote the solutions of (
3.1
)–(
3.6
)
as
(\rho^{\varepsilon,b},u^{\varepsilon,b})
for simplicity of presentation
in this section, since
\rho^{\varepsilon,b}>0
on
[0,T]\times[a,b(t)]
for fixed
b>0
.

To take the limit, we have to be careful, since the weak solutions may involve the vacuum.
We use the similar compactness arguments as in
[
62
,
31
]
to handle
the limit:
b\rightarrow\infty
.
First of all, we understand our solutions
(\rho^{\varepsilon,b},u^{\varepsilon,b})
to be the zero extension
of
(\rho^{\varepsilon,b},u^{\varepsilon,b})
on
\big([0,T]\times[0,\infty)\big)\backslash\Omega_{T}
.
It follows from Lemma
3.4
that

(4.1)

\lim_{b\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}b(t)=\infty,

which implies that domain
[0,T]\times[a,b(t)]
expands to
[0,T]\times(0,\infty)

as
b\to\infty
.
That is, for any compact set
K\Subset(0,\infty)
, when
b\gg 1
,

K\Subset(a,b(t))
for all
t\in[0,T]
.

Now we define

(4.2)

{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon,b}(t,\textbf{x}):=m^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r)\frac{\textbf{x}}{r}=(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b})(t,r)\frac{\textbf{x}}{r}.

Then it is direct to check that the corresponding vector function
(\rho^{\varepsilon,b},{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon,b},\Phi^{\varepsilon,b})

is a classical solution of CNSPEs
for
(t,\textbf{x})\in[0,\infty)\times\Omega_{t}
:

(4.3)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}+\mbox{div}\,{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon,b}=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon,b}+\mbox{div}\big(\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon,b}\otimes{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon,b}}{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}\big)+\nabla p(\rho^{\varepsilon,b})=-\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\nabla\Phi^{\varepsilon,b}+\varepsilon\mbox{div}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}D(\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon,b}}{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}})\big),\\[5.69054pt]
\Delta\Phi^{\varepsilon,b}=\kappa\rho^{\varepsilon,b},\end{cases}

with
{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon,b}\Big|_{\textbf{x}\in\partial B_{a}(\mathbf{0})}={\bf 0}
.

4.1
Taking limit
b\rightarrow\infty

Lemma 4.1

For fixed
\varepsilon>0
, there exists a function
\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)
such that, as
b\rightarrow\infty

(
up to a subsequence
)
,

(4.4)

(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}},\,\rho^{\varepsilon,b})\longrightarrow(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}},\,\rho^{\varepsilon})\quad\mbox{ {\it a.e.} and strongly in}\ C(0,T;L^{q}_{\rm loc})

for any
q\in[1,\infty)
, where
L^{q}_{\rm loc}
denotes
L^{q}(K)
for
K\Subset(0,\infty)
.

Proof.

It follows from Lemmas
3.1
and
3.3
that

(4.5)

\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}_{\rm loc})\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\infty}_{\rm loc})\qquad\mbox{uniformly in $b>0$}.

Using the mass equation
\eqref{2.1}_{1}
and Lemma
3.1
, we have

\displaystyle-\partial_{t}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}

\displaystyle=\Big(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}\Big)_{r}u^{\varepsilon,b}+\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}+\frac{n-1}{2r}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b}

\displaystyle=\Big(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b}\Big)_{r}-\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}+\frac{n-1}{2r}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b}\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{-1}_{\rm loc})

uniformly in
b>0
,
which, together with the Aubin-Lions lemma, yields that

\displaystyle\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}\quad\mbox{is compact in $C(0,T;L^{q}_{\rm loc})$ for any $q\in[1,\infty)$}.

Notice that, for any
K\Subset(0,\infty)
and
b_{1},b_{2}\in(1,\infty)
,

|\rho^{\varepsilon,b_{1}}(t,r)-\rho^{\varepsilon,b_{2}}(t,r)|\leq C_{T,K}\Big|\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b_{1}}}-\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b_{2}}}\Big|\qquad\mbox{for any $(t,r)\in[0,T]\times K$},

where
C_{T,K}>0
is a constant independent of
b_{1}
and
b_{2}
.
Then there exists a function
\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)
such that, as
b\rightarrow\infty

(
up to a subsequence
)
,

(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}},\rho^{\varepsilon,b})\longrightarrow(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}},\rho^{\varepsilon})
a.e. and strongly in
C(0,T;L^{q}_{\rm loc})
for any
q\in[1,\infty)
.
Then (
4.4
) follows.
∎

Corollary 4.2

For fixed
\varepsilon>0
, the pressure function sequence
p(\rho^{\varepsilon,b})
is uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{q}_{\rm loc})
for all
q\in[1,\infty]

and, as
b\rightarrow\infty

(
up to a subsequence
)
,

\displaystyle p(\rho^{\varepsilon,b})\longrightarrow p(\rho^{\varepsilon})\qquad\mbox{strongly in $L^{q}(0,T;L^{q}_{\rm loc})$ for all $q\in[1,\infty)$}.

Lemma 4.3

For fixed
\varepsilon>0
, as
b\rightarrow\infty

(
up to a subsequence
)
,
the momentum function sequence
m^{\varepsilon,b}:=\rho^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b}
converges strongly in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{q}_{\rm loc})
to
some function
m^{\varepsilon}(t,r)
for all
q\in[1,\infty)
.
In particular, we have

\displaystyle m^{\varepsilon,b}=\rho^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b}\longrightarrow m^{\varepsilon}(t,r)\qquad\mbox{a.e. in $[0,T]\times(0,\infty)$}.

Proof.

Notice that
\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\infty}_{\rm loc})

and
\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}_{\rm loc})
in
b>0
,
which imply that

(4.6)

\displaystyle\rho^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b}=\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}\,\Big(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b}\Big)\quad\mbox{is uniformly bounded in}\ L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}_{\rm loc}).

A direct calculation shows that

(4.7)

\displaystyle(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b})_{r}

\displaystyle=\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{r}u^{\varepsilon,b}+\rho^{\varepsilon,b}u_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}

\displaystyle=2\Big(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}\Big)_{r}\,\Big(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b}\Big)+\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}\,\Big(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}\Big)

is uniformly bounded in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{1}_{\rm loc})
.
Then it follows from (
4.6
)–(
4.7
) that

(4.8)

\displaystyle\rho^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b}\qquad\mbox{is uniformly bounded in $L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,1}_{\rm loc})$}.

It follows from (
4.5
) and Lemma
3.1
that

(4.9)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\partial_{r}\Big(\Big(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b}\Big)^{2}\Big)\in L^{\infty}(0,T;W_{\rm loc}^{-1,1}),\\[7.11317pt]
\displaystyle\frac{n-1}{r}\Big(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b}\Big)^{2}\in L^{\infty}(0,T;L_{\rm loc}^{1}),\\[7.11317pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{r}p(\rho^{\varepsilon,b})\in L^{2}(0,T;H_{\rm loc}^{-1}),\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\kappa\frac{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}{r^{n-1}}\int_{a}^{r}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\in L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\infty}_{\rm loc}),\end{cases}

and

(4.10)

\displaystyle\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}\,\Big(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b}_{r}\Big)+\frac{n-1}{r}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}\,\Big(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b}\Big)\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}_{\rm loc})

uniformly in
b>0
.

Therefore, it follows from (
4.10
) that

(4.11)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(u_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}+\frac{n-1}{r}u^{\varepsilon,b})\big)\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{-1}_{\rm loc})\qquad\mbox{uniformly in $b$}.

Also, using Lemmas
3.1
and
3.3
, we have

(4.12)

\displaystyle\frac{n-1}{r}u^{\varepsilon,b}\partial_{r}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}

\displaystyle=\frac{2(n-1)}{r}\Big(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}\Big)_{r}\,\Big(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b}\Big)\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{1}_{\rm loc})\,\,\,\mbox{uniformly in $b$}.

Thus, substituting (
4.9
) and (
4.11
)–(
4.12
) into
\eqref{2.1}_{2}
yields that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b})\in L^{2}(0,T;W^{-1,1}_{\rm loc})\qquad\mbox{uniformly in $b>0$},

which, together with (
4.8
) and the Aubin-Lions lemma,
implies that

\displaystyle\rho^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b}\qquad\mbox{is compact in $L^{2}(0,T;L^{q}_{\rm loc})$ for all $q\in[1,\infty)$}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 4.4

The limit function
m^{\varepsilon}(t,r)
in Lemma
4.3
satisfies that

m^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0
a.e. on
\{(t,r)\,:\,\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0\}
. Furthermore, there exists
a function
u^{\varepsilon}(t,r)
such that
m^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)u^{\varepsilon}(t,r)
a.e., and
\,u^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0
a.e.
on
\{(t,r)\,:\,\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0\}
. Moreover, as
b\rightarrow\infty

(
up to a subsequence
)
,

\displaystyle m^{\varepsilon,b}\longrightarrow m^{\varepsilon}=\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon}\qquad\mbox{strongly in $L^{2}(0,T;L^{q}_{\rm loc})$ for $q\in[1,\infty)$},

\displaystyle\frac{m^{\varepsilon,b}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}}\longrightarrow\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}u^{\varepsilon}=:\frac{m^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\qquad\mbox{strongly in $L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}_{\rm loc})$}.

Proof.

We divide the proof into three steps.

1. We first claim that
m^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0\,\,

a.e.
on
\{(t,r)\,:\,\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0\}
.

To prove this claim, for any given
T>0
and
0<d<D<\infty
, we define

V:=\big\{(t,r)\in[0,T]\times[d,D]\,:\,\mbox{$\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0$ and $m^{\varepsilon}(t,r)\neq 0$}\big\}\setminus\mathcal{N},

where
\mathcal{N}
is the set where the subsequence (still denoted)
(\rho^{\varepsilon,b},m^{\varepsilon,b})
does not converge to
(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})

so that the Lebesgue measure
of
\mathcal{N}
must be zero:
|\mathcal{N}|=0
, since
(\rho^{\varepsilon,b},m^{\varepsilon,b})
converges to
(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})

a.e.
as
b\to\infty
.

If
|V|=0
, then we have done. If
|V|>0
, then it is clear that

(4.13)

\displaystyle\liminf_{b\to\infty}\frac{|m^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r)|^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r)}=\lim_{b\to\infty}\frac{|m^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r)|^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r)}=\infty\qquad\mbox{for}\,\,(t,r)\in V.

On the other hand, notice that
\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b}r^{\frac{n-1}{2}}=\frac{m^{\varepsilon,b}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}}r^{\frac{n-1}{2}}

is uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2})
. Then Fatou’s lemma implies that

(4.14)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\liminf\limits_{b\to\infty}\frac{|m^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r)|^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r)}\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq\liminf\limits_{b\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\frac{|m^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r)|^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r)}\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\leq C(T,E_{0},M)<\infty.

Combining (
4.13
) with (
4.14
) yields

(4.15)

\displaystyle\infty

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\liminf\limits_{b\to\infty}\frac{|m^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r)|^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r)}\,{\mathbf{1}}_{V}(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\liminf\limits_{b\to\infty}\frac{|m^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r)|^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r)}\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\leq C(T,E_{0},M)<\infty,

which is impossible,
where
{\mathbf{1}}_{V}(t,r)
is the indicator function of set
V
.
Therefore, it must be that
|V|=0
, which leads to the claim.

2. Now we can define velocity
u^{\varepsilon}(t,r)
as

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}(t,r):=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{m^{\varepsilon}(t,r)}{\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)}\quad{\it a.e.}\,\,\,\mbox{on}\,\,\{(t,r)\,:\,\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)\neq 0\},\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle 0,\qquad\quad\,\,\,\,{\it a.e.}\,\,\,\mbox{on}\,\,\{(t,r)\,:\,\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0\},\end{cases}

and define

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{m^{\varepsilon}(t,r)}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)}}:=0\qquad{\it a.e.}\,\,\mbox{on}\,\,\{(t,r)\,:\,\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0\}.

Then it is clear that

(4.16)

\displaystyle m^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)u^{\varepsilon}(t,r)\,\,\,\,\,a.e.,\quad\,\,\,\frac{m^{\varepsilon}(t,r)}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)}u^{\varepsilon}(t,r)\,\,\,\,\,a.e.

It follows from (
4.14
), (
4.16
), and Lemmas
4.1
and
4.3
that

(4.17)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\rho^{\varepsilon}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\rho^{\varepsilon}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\,\mathbf{1}_{\{\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)>0\}}\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\frac{|m^{\varepsilon}|^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\,\mathbf{1}_{\{\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)>0\}}\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\liminf\limits_{b\to\infty}\frac{|m^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r)|^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r)}\,\mathbf{1}_{\{\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)>0\}}\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\liminf\limits_{b\to\infty}\frac{|m^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r)|^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r)}\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(T,E_{0},M)<\infty,

where
\mathbf{1}_{\{\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)>0\}}
is the indicator function of set
\{\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)>0\}
.
Similarly, it follows from Lemma
3.7
and Fatou’s lemma that

(4.18)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\rho^{\varepsilon}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{3}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{b\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}|u^{\varepsilon,b}|^{3}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,D,M,E_{0},T)<\infty.

3. Next, since
(\rho^{\varepsilon,b},m^{\varepsilon,b})
converges
a.e.
, it is direct to know that
sequence
\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b}=\frac{m^{\varepsilon,b}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}}
converges
a.e.

to
\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}u^{\varepsilon}=\frac{m^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}
on
\{(t,r)\,:\,\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)\neq 0\}
.
Moreover, for any given positive constant
k\geq 1
, we have

(4.19)

\displaystyle\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b}\mathbf{1}_{\{|u^{\varepsilon,b}|\leq k\}}\longrightarrow\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}u^{\varepsilon}\mathbf{1}_{\{|u^{\varepsilon}|\leq k\}}\quad\mbox{\it a.e.}.

It is direct to know that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\Big(\big|\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b}\big|^{\frac{12}{5}}+|\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}u^{\varepsilon}|^{\frac{12}{5}}\Big)\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}|u^{\varepsilon,b}|^{3}+(\rho^{\varepsilon,b})^{\gamma+1}+\rho^{\varepsilon}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{3}+(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma+1}\big)\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,D,M,E_{0},T),

which, together with (
4.19
), yields that

(4.20)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\Big|\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b}\mathbf{1}_{\{|u^{\varepsilon,b}|\leq k\}}-\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}u^{\varepsilon}\mathbf{1}_{\{|u^{\varepsilon}|\leq k\}}\Big|^{2}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\longrightarrow 0\,\,\,\,\mbox{as $b\rightarrow\infty$}.

For
k\geq 1
, using (
4.18
) and Lemma
3.7
, we have

(4.21)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\Big(\big|\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b}\mathbf{1}_{\{|u^{\varepsilon,b}|\geq k\}}\big|^{2}+\big|\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}u^{\varepsilon}\mathbf{1}_{\{|u^{\varepsilon}|\geq k\}}\big|^{2}\Big)\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{k}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}|u^{\varepsilon,b}|^{3}+\rho^{\varepsilon}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{3}\big)\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(d,D,M,E_{0},T)}{k}.

Notice that

(4.22)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\Big|\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b}-\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}u^{\varepsilon}\Big|^{2}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\Big|\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b}\mathbf{1}_{\{|u^{\varepsilon,b}|\leq k\}}-\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}u^{\varepsilon}\mathbf{1}_{\{|u^{\varepsilon}|\leq k\}}\Big|^{2}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\Big|\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b}\mathbf{1}_{\{|u^{\varepsilon,b}|\geq k\}}\Big|^{2}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\Big|\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}u^{\varepsilon}\mathbf{1}_{\{|u^{\varepsilon}|\geq k\}}\Big|^{2}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t.

Substituting (
4.20
)–(
4.21
) into (
4.22
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{b\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\Big|\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b}-\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}u^{\varepsilon}\Big|^{2}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\leq\frac{C(d,D,M,E_{0},T)}{k}\quad\,\,\mbox{for all $k\geq 1$}.

Thus, by taking
k\rightarrow\infty
, we have proved that

\frac{m^{\varepsilon,b}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}}\longrightarrow\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}u^{\varepsilon}\equiv\frac{m^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}

strongly in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}_{\rm loc})
. Therefore, the proof of Lemma
4.4
is complete.
∎

Let
(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})
be the limit obtained above.
First, using (
3.16
), (
4.1
),
Lemmas
3.1
,
3.3
,
3.5
,
3.7
,
4.1
, and
4.4
,
Corollary 3.2, Fatou’s lemma, and the lower semicontinuity, we have

Proposition 4.5

Under assumptions (
3.11
)–(
3.12
),
for any fixed
\varepsilon
and
T>0
, the limit functions
(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})=(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon})
satisfy

(4.23)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\hskip 14.22636pt\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)\geq 0\,\,\,\,\,a.e.,\end{split}

(4.24)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636ptu^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0,\,\,\big(\frac{m^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\big)(t,r)=\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}(t,r)u^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pta.e.\,\,\mbox{\, on $\{(t,r)\,:\,\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0\}$},

(4.25)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\hskip 14.22636pt\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r\leq\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}\qquad\,\,\mbox{for all $t\geq 0$},\end{split}

(4.26)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(\frac{1}{2}\rho^{\varepsilon}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}+(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma}\big)(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r+\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{-n+1}\Big(\int_{0}^{r}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\Big)^{2}\,{\rm d}r

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int_{0}^{r}\rho^{\varepsilon}z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\Big)\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)\,r{\rm d}r+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}(\rho^{\varepsilon}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{2})(s,r)\,r^{n-3}{\rm d}r{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(M,E_{0})\qquad\mbox{for all $t\geq 0$},

(4.27)

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)})_{r}\big|^{2}\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|\big((\rho^{\varepsilon}(s,r))^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\big)_{r}\big|^{2}\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\quad\leq C(M,E_{0},T)\qquad\,\,\mbox{for $t\in[0,T]$},

(4.28)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{3}+(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma+\theta}+(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma+1}\big)(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\leq C(d,D,M,E_{0},T),\end{split}

where
[d,D]\Subset(0,\infty)
.

The following lemma is devoted to the convergence of the potential functions
\Phi^{\varepsilon,b}
.

Lemma 4.6

For fixed
\varepsilon>0
, there exists a function
\Phi^{\varepsilon}(t,\mathbf{x})=\Phi^{\varepsilon}(t,r)
such that,
as
b\rightarrow\infty

(
up to a subsequence
)
,

(4.29)

\displaystyle\Phi^{\varepsilon,b}\rightharpoonup\Phi^{\varepsilon}\quad\mbox{weak-$\ast$ in $L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))$ and weakly in $L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))$},

(4.30)

\displaystyle\Phi_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r)r^{n-1}\longrightarrow\Phi_{r}^{\varepsilon}(t,r)r^{n-1}=\kappa\int_{0}^{r}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\quad\mbox{in $C_{\rm loc}([0,T]\times[0,\infty))$},

and

(4.31)

\displaystyle\|\Phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{n-2}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|\nabla\Phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C(M,E_{0})\qquad\mbox{for $t\geq 0$}.

Moreover, if
\gamma>\frac{2n}{n+2}
,

(4.32)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}|(\Phi_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}-\Phi_{r}^{\varepsilon})(t,r)|^{2}\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r\longrightarrow 0\quad\mbox{as $b\to\infty\,$ {\rm(}\mbox{up to a subsequence}{\rm)}}.

Proof.

The proof of (
4.29
) and (
4.31
) are direct by using Corollary
3.2
.

We now prove (
4.30
). For any
D>0
and
(t,r)\in[0,T]\times[0,D]
,
taking
b
sufficiently large, then it follows from (
3.15
) that

(4.33)

\displaystyle\Big|\Phi_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r)r^{n-1}-\kappa\int_{0}^{r}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\Big|

\displaystyle=\left|\kappa\int_{0}^{D}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\big)(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\int_{\sigma}^{D}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\big)(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\right|+C\left|\int_{0}^{\sigma}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\big)(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\right|.

Using (
4.4
), we see that, for any fixed
\sigma>0
,

(4.34)

\displaystyle\lim_{b\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left|\int_{\sigma}^{D}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\big)(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\right|=0.

It follows from (
4.23
) and Lemma
3.1
that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{\sigma}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\big)(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\int_{0}^{\sigma}\big((\rho^{\varepsilon,b})^{\gamma}+(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma}\big)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}r\Big)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\,\Big(\int_{0}^{\sigma}z^{n-1}{\rm d}r\Big)^{1-\frac{1}{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq C(M,E_{0})\sigma^{n(1-\frac{1}{\gamma})}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as $\sigma\rightarrow 0$},

which, together with (
4.33
)–(
4.34
), yields that

\sup_{[0,T]\times[0,D]}\Big|\Phi_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r)r^{n-1}-\kappa\int_{0}^{r}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\Big|\longrightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as $b\rightarrow\infty$},

which leads to (
4.30
).

For (
4.32
), we first notice that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{n-1}}\Big|\int_{0}^{r}(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon})(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\Big|^{2}\leq C(M)r^{-n+1}\qquad\mbox{for $r>0$},

which yields that

(4.35)

\displaystyle\int_{k}^{\infty}\frac{1}{r^{n-1}}\Big|\int_{0}^{r}(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon})(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\Big|^{2}{\rm d}r\leq C(M)k^{-n+2}.

Using the Hölder inequality, we have

(4.36)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{n-1}}\Big|\int_{0}^{r}(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon})(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{-n+1}\left(\int_{0}^{r}\big((\rho^{\varepsilon,b})^{\gamma}+(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma}\big)z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\right)^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}\,r^{2n(1-\frac{1}{\gamma})}

\displaystyle\leq C(E_{0},M)r^{n+1-\frac{2n}{\gamma}}.

Since
n+1-\frac{2n}{\gamma}>-1
for
\gamma>\frac{2n}{n+2}
, it follows from (
4.4
), (
4.36
),
and Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem that, for any given
k>0
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{k}\frac{1}{r^{n-1}}\Big|\int_{0}^{r}(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon})(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\Big|^{2}{\rm d}r\longrightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as $b\to\infty$},

which, together with (
4.35
), yields that

\displaystyle\lim_{b\to\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}|(\Phi_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}-\Phi_{r}^{\varepsilon})(t,r)|^{2}\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r

\displaystyle=\lim_{b\to\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{r^{n-1}}\Big|\int_{0}^{r}(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon})(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\Big|^{2}{\rm d}r

\displaystyle\leq C(M)k^{-n+2}+\lim_{b\to\infty}\int_{0}^{k}\frac{1}{r^{n-1}}\Big|\int_{0}^{r}(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon})(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\Big|^{2}{\rm d}r

\displaystyle\leq C(M)k^{-n+2}.

Then (
4.32
) follows by taking
k\to\infty
.
∎

Remark 4.7
.

The convergence result (
4.32
) is essential to prove the energy inequality for the case that
\kappa=1

(
gaseous stars
)
.
Moreover, from (
4.30
), it is direct to know that
\Phi^{\varepsilon}
satisfies the Poisson equation in the classical sense except the origin:

\Delta\Phi^{\varepsilon}(t,\mathbf{x})=\kappa\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,\mathbf{x})\qquad\mbox{for}\ (t,\mathbf{x})\in[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{\mathbf{0}\}.

Lemma 4.8

Let
\gamma>1
for
\kappa=-1

(
plasmas
)
and
\gamma>\frac{2n}{n+2}
for
\kappa=1

(
gaseous stars
)
. Then

(4.37)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{m^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\Big|^{2}+\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})\Big)(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r-\frac{\kappa}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}|\Phi^{\varepsilon}(t,r)|^{2}\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{m_{0}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}}}\Big|^{2}+\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}e(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon})\Big)(r)\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r-\frac{\kappa}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}|\Phi_{0}^{\varepsilon}(r)|^{2}\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r.

Proof.

For
\kappa=-1

(
plasmas
)
, (
4.37
) follows directly from (
3.20
), (
3.33
), and Fatou’s lemma.

For
\kappa=1

(
gaseous stars
)
, (
4.37
) follows from (
3.20
), (
3.33
), (
4.32
), and Fatou’s lemma,
where the strong convergence of the gravitational potentials (
4.32
) plays a key role.
∎

Denote

(\rho^{\varepsilon},{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon},\Phi^{\varepsilon})(t,\mathbf{x}):=(\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r),\ m^{\varepsilon}(t,r)\frac{\mathbf{x}}{r},\ \Phi^{\varepsilon}(t,r)).

We show that
(\rho^{\varepsilon},{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon},\Phi^{\varepsilon})
is a global weak solution of the Cauchy problem for CNSPEs (
1.4
)
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
in the sense of Definition
2.6
.

Lemma 4.9

Let
0\leq t_{1}<t_{2}\leq T
, and let
\zeta(t,\mathbf{x})\in C^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
be any smooth function with compact support.
Then

(4.38)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t_{2},\mathbf{x})\zeta(t_{2},\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t_{1},\mathbf{x})\zeta(t_{1},\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}\zeta_{t}+{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\zeta\big)\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t.

Moreover, the total mass is conserved:

(4.39)

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}=M\qquad\,\mbox{for $t\geq 0$}.

Proof.

Using (
3.65
), we can choose sufficiently large
b\gg 1
so that

{\rm supp}\,\zeta(t,\cdot)\subset B_{b/2}(\mathbf{0})
for
t\in[0,T]
.
Then it follows from
\eqref{5.5-2}_{1}
and a direct calculation that

(4.40)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{a}(\mathbf{0})}\big((\rho^{\varepsilon,b})_{t}+\mbox{div}\,{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon,b}\big)\zeta(t,\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{a}(\mathbf{0})}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\zeta\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\bigg|_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}-\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{a}(\mathbf{0})}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\zeta_{t}+{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon,b}\cdot\nabla\zeta\big)\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\zeta\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\bigg|_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}-\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\zeta_{t}+{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon,b}\cdot\nabla\zeta\big)\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t,

where we have used the fact that
(\rho^{\varepsilon,b},m^{\varepsilon,b})
is extended by zero in
[0,T]\times[0,a)
.

Notice that, for
i=1,2
,

(4.41)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\big)(t_{i},\mathbf{x})\zeta(t_{i},\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{\sigma}(\mathbf{0})}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\big)(t_{i},\mathbf{x})\zeta(t_{i},\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\Big|\int_{B_{\sigma}(\mathbf{0})}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\big)(t_{i},\mathbf{x})\zeta(t_{i},\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\Big|.

We denote

(4.42)

\displaystyle\phi(t,r):=\int_{\partial B_{1}(\mathbf{0})}\zeta(t,r\boldsymbol{\omega})\,{\rm d}\boldsymbol{\omega}\in C^{1}_{0}([0,T]\times[0,\infty)),

which, together with (
4.4
), yields that, for any fixed
\sigma>0
,

(4.43)

\displaystyle\lim_{b\rightarrow\infty}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{\sigma}(\mathbf{0})}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\big)(t_{i},\mathbf{x})\zeta(t_{i},\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\Big|

\displaystyle=\lim_{b\rightarrow\infty}\Big|\int_{\sigma}^{\infty}\omega_{n}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\big)(t_{i},r)\phi(t_{i},r)\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r\Big|=0.

Using Lemma
3.1
and (
4.23
), we have

(4.44)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{B_{\sigma}(\mathbf{0})}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\big)(t_{i},\mathbf{x})\zeta(t_{i},\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\zeta\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big\{\int_{0}^{\sigma}\big((\rho^{\varepsilon,b})^{\gamma}+(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma}\big)\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r\Big\}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\,\Big\{\int_{0}^{\sigma}r^{n-1}{\rm d}r\Big\}^{1-\frac{1}{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq C(M,E_{0})\|\zeta\|_{L^{\infty}}\sigma^{n(1-\frac{1}{\gamma})}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as $\sigma\rightarrow 0$},

which, together with (
4.41
) and (
4.43
), leads to

(4.45)

\displaystyle\lim_{b\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t_{i},\mathbf{x})\zeta(t_{i},\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t_{i},\mathbf{x})\zeta(t_{i},\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\qquad\mbox{for $i=1,2$. }

From (
4.42
), it is direct to show that

(4.46)

\displaystyle\phi_{r}(t,r)=\int_{\partial B_{1}(\mathbf{0})}\boldsymbol{\omega}\cdot\nabla\zeta(t,r\boldsymbol{\omega})\,{\rm d}\boldsymbol{\omega},

which, together with (
4.4
) and Lemma
4.4
, implies that

(4.47)

\displaystyle\lim_{b\rightarrow\infty}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{\sigma}(\mathbf{0})}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\zeta_{t}+{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon,b}\cdot\nabla\zeta\big)\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\lim_{b\rightarrow\infty}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\sigma}^{\infty}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\phi_{t}+m^{\varepsilon,b}\phi_{r}\big)\,\omega_{n}r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\sigma}^{\infty}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}\phi_{t}+m^{\varepsilon}\phi_{r}\big)\,\omega_{n}r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{\sigma}(\mathbf{0})}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}\zeta_{t}+{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\zeta\big)\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t.

Similar to those as in (
4.44
), we have

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{B_{\sigma}(\mathbf{0})}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\big)\zeta_{t}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t\Big|\leq C(E_{0},T)\|\zeta_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\,\sigma^{n(1-\frac{1}{\gamma})},

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{B_{\sigma}(\mathbf{0})}\big({\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon,b}-{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cdot\nabla\zeta\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big\{\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{0}^{\sigma}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}+\rho^{\varepsilon}\big)(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\Big\}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\Big\{\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{0}^{\sigma}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}|u^{\varepsilon,b}|^{2}+\rho^{\varepsilon}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\big)(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\Big\}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(M,E_{0},T)\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{\infty}}\sigma^{\frac{n}{2}(1-\frac{1}{\gamma})},

which, together with (
4.47
), yields that

(4.48)

\displaystyle\lim_{b\rightarrow\infty}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\zeta_{t}+{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon,b}\cdot\nabla\zeta\big)\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}\zeta_{t}+{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\zeta\big){\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t.

Combining (
4.40
) with (
4.45
) and (
4.48
), we conclude (
4.38
).

Finally, we prove the conservation of mass (
4.39
).
We take smooth test functions
\zeta(t,\mathbf{x})=\phi_{k}(r)
in (
4.38
) with

(4.49)

\displaystyle\phi_{k}(r)=\begin{cases}1\qquad&\mbox{for $r\in[0,k]$},\\
\mbox{smooth}\quad&\mbox{for $r\in[k,k+1]$},\\
0\qquad&\mbox{for $r\in[k+1,\infty)$},\end{cases}

(4.50)

\displaystyle|\phi_{k}^{\prime}(r)|\leq C\qquad\mbox{for all $r\in[0,\infty)$},

where
C>0
is a constant independent of
k
.
Now it follows from (
4.38
) and (
4.49
)–(
4.50
) that

(4.51)

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,\mathbf{x})\phi_{k}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})\phi_{k}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{k\leq|\mathbf{x}|\leq k+1}{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\phi_{k}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}s.

From (
2.2
), (
4.25
), and Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem, we have

(4.52)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,\mathbf{x}),\,\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x}))\phi_{k}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,\mathbf{x}),\,\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x}))\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}.

Since

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{k\leq|\mathbf{x}|\leq k+1}{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\phi_{k}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{k}^{k+1}\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon}\phi_{k}^{\prime}(r)\,\omega_{n}\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left\{\int_{0}^{T}\int_{k}^{k+1}\rho^{\varepsilon}\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\left\{\int_{0}^{T}\int_{k}^{k+1}\rho^{\varepsilon}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}\longrightarrow 0\,\,\,\,\,\mbox{as $k\rightarrow\infty$},

together with (
4.51
)–(
4.52
), we conclude (
4.39
).
∎

Lemma 4.10

Let
\boldsymbol{\psi}(t,\mathbf{x})\in\left(C^{2}_{0}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n})\right)^{n}
be any smooth function with compact support
so that
\boldsymbol{\psi}(T,\mathbf{x})={\bf 0}
. Then

(4.53)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}\Big\{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\psi}+\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\big(\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\nabla\big)\boldsymbol{\psi}+p(\rho^{\varepsilon})\,\mbox{\rm div}\,\boldsymbol{\psi}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\nabla\Phi^{\varepsilon}\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi}\Big\}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi}(0,\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=-\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}\Big\{\frac{1}{2}{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\big(\Delta\boldsymbol{\psi}+\nabla\mbox{\rm div}\,\boldsymbol{\psi}\big)+\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\big(\nabla\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\cdot\nabla\big)\boldsymbol{\psi}

\displaystyle\hskip 156.49014pt\,\,\,\,+\nabla\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\cdot\big(\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\nabla\big)\boldsymbol{\psi}\Big\}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\Big\{V^{\varepsilon}\frac{\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{x}}{r^{2}}+\frac{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}{r}\frac{m^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\big(I_{n\times n}-\frac{\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{x}}{r^{2}}\big)\Big\}:\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t,

where
V^{\varepsilon}(t,r)\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
is a function such that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|V^{\varepsilon}(t,\mathbf{x})|^{2}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t\leq C(E_{0},M)\quad\mbox{for some $C(E_{0},M)>0$ independent of $T>0$}.

Proof.

For any given
\sigma\in(0,1]
, let
\chi_{\sigma}(r)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
be a cut-off function satisfying

(4.54)

\displaystyle\chi_{\sigma}(r)=0\,\,\,\mbox{for $r\leq\sigma$};\,\,\,\,\,\chi_{\sigma}(r)=1\,\,\,\mbox{for $r\geq 2\sigma$};\,\,\,\,\,|\chi_{\sigma}^{\prime}(r)|+\sigma|\chi_{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}(r)|\leq\frac{C}{\sigma}\,\,\,\mbox{for $r\in\mathbb{R}$}.

Denote
\Psi_{\sigma}(t,\textbf{x})=\boldsymbol{\psi}(t,\textbf{x})\chi_{\sigma}(|\textbf{x}|)
.
Taking
b\gg 1
large enough so that
a=b^{-1}\leq\sigma
,
then it follows from
\eqref{5.5-2}_{2}
and integration by parts that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}\Big\{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon,b}\cdot\partial_{t}\Psi_{\sigma}+\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon,b}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}}\cdot\big(\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon,b}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}}\cdot\nabla\big)\Psi_{\sigma}+p(\rho^{\varepsilon,b})\,\mbox{\rm div}\,\Psi_{\sigma}\Big\}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}\cdot\Psi_{\sigma}(0,\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\textbf{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\nabla\Phi^{\varepsilon,b}\cdot\Psi_{\sigma}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t+R^{\varepsilon,b},

where

\displaystyle R^{\varepsilon,b}

\displaystyle=-\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}\Big\{\frac{1}{2}{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon,b}\cdot\big(\Delta\Psi_{\sigma}+\nabla\mbox{div}\,\Psi_{\sigma}\big)+\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon,b}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}}\cdot\big(\nabla\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}\cdot\nabla\big)\Psi_{\sigma}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\,\,\,+\nabla\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}\cdot\big(\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon,b}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}}\cdot\nabla\big)\Psi_{\sigma}\Big\}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}\Big(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}D(\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon,b}}{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}})\Big):\nabla\Psi_{\sigma}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t.

For the term involving the potentials, using (
4.4
) and (
4.29
), we have

(4.55)

\displaystyle\lim_{b\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\nabla\Phi^{\varepsilon,b}\cdot\Psi_{\sigma}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}\rho^{\varepsilon}\nabla\Phi^{\varepsilon}\cdot\Psi_{\sigma}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t.

For the convergence of the viscous term, it follows from (
4.2
) and a direct calculation that

\displaystyle\partial_{i}\Big(\frac{\mathcal{M}^{\varepsilon,b}_{j}}{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}\Big)=u^{\varepsilon,b}_{r}\frac{x_{i}x_{j}}{r^{2}}+\frac{u^{\varepsilon,b}}{r}\big(\delta_{ij}-\frac{x_{i}x_{j}}{r^{2}}\big).

Thus, using Lemma
3.1
, there exists a function
V^{\varepsilon}(t,r)
so that

(4.56)

\displaystyle\sqrt{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}D\Big(\frac{\mathcal{M}^{\varepsilon,b}_{j}}{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}\Big)\,{\rm-\!\!\!\rightharpoonup}\,V^{\varepsilon}\frac{{\mathbf{x}}\otimes{\mathbf{x}}}{r^{2}}+\frac{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}{r}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}u^{\varepsilon}\Big(I_{n\times n}-\frac{{\mathbf{x}}\otimes{\mathbf{x}}}{r^{2}}\Big)

in
L^{2}(0,T;(L^{2}(B_{\sigma^{-1}}(\mathbf{0})\backslash B_{\sigma}(\mathbf{0})))^{n\times n})
as
b\rightarrow\infty
for any given
\sigma>0
, and

(4.57)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|V^{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t\leq C(E_{0},M).

Denote

\displaystyle\phi_{1\sigma}(t,r):=\int_{\partial B_{1}(\mathbf{0})}\big\{\boldsymbol{\omega}\cdot(\Delta\Psi_{\sigma})(t,r\boldsymbol{\omega})+\boldsymbol{\omega}\cdot(\nabla\mbox{div}\,\Psi_{\sigma})(t,r\boldsymbol{\omega})\big\}\,{\rm d}\boldsymbol{\omega}.

Then
\phi_{1\sigma}\in C_{0}([0,T]\times(0,\infty))
. Hence, using Lemma
4.4
, we find that, as
b\rightarrow\infty
,

(4.58)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon,b}\cdot\big\{\Delta\Psi_{\sigma}+\nabla\mbox{div}\,\Psi_{\sigma}\big\}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}m^{\varepsilon,b}\phi_{1\sigma}\,\omega_{n}r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}m^{\varepsilon}\phi_{1\sigma}\,\omega_{n}r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\big\{\Delta\Psi_{\sigma}+\nabla\mbox{div}\,\Psi_{\sigma}\big\}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t.

Similarly, using Lemmas
4.1
and
4.4
, we see that, as
b\rightarrow\infty
,

(4.59)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\Big\{\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon,b}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}}\cdot\big(\nabla\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}\cdot\nabla\big)\Psi_{\sigma}+\nabla\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}\cdot\big(\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon,b}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}}\cdot\nabla\big)\Psi_{\sigma}\Big\}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\Big\{\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\big(\nabla\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\cdot\nabla\big)\Psi_{\sigma}+\nabla\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\cdot\big(\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\nabla\big)\Psi_{\sigma}\Big\}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t.

Combining (
4.56
) and (
4.58
)–(
4.59
), we obtain that, as
b\rightarrow\infty
,

(4.60)

\displaystyle R^{\varepsilon,b}\longrightarrow

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}\Big\{\frac{1}{2}{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\big(\Delta\Psi_{\sigma}+\nabla\mbox{div}\,\Psi_{\sigma}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 51.21504pt+\Big(\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\big(\nabla\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\cdot\nabla\big)+\nabla\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\cdot\big(\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\nabla\big)\Big)\Psi_{\sigma}\Big\}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\Big\{V^{\varepsilon}\frac{\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{x}}{r^{2}}+\frac{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}{r}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}u^{\varepsilon}\Big(I_{n\times n}-\frac{\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{x}}{r^{2}}\Big)\Big\}:\nabla\Psi_{\sigma}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t.

Also, by similar arguments as in (
4.58
), using Lemma
4.1
, Corollary
4.2
,
and Lemma
4.4
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}\Big\{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon,b}\cdot\partial_{t}\Psi_{\sigma}+\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon,b}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}}\cdot\big(\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon,b}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}}\cdot\nabla\big)\Psi_{\sigma}+p(\rho^{\varepsilon,b})\,\mbox{\rm div}\,\Psi_{\sigma}\Big\}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}\cdot\Psi_{\sigma}(0,\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}\Big\{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{t}\Psi_{\sigma}+\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\big(\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\nabla\big)\Psi_{\sigma}+p(\rho^{\varepsilon})\,\mbox{\rm div}\,\Psi_{\sigma}\Big\}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\Psi_{\sigma}(0,\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}

as
b\rightarrow\infty
, which, together with (
4.55
) and (
4.60
), yields that

(4.61)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}\Big\{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{t}\Psi_{\sigma}+\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\big(\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\nabla\big)\Psi_{\sigma}+p(\rho^{\varepsilon})\,\mbox{\rm div}\,\Psi_{\sigma}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\nabla\Phi^{\varepsilon}\cdot\Psi_{\sigma}\Big\}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\Psi_{\sigma}(0,\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=-\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}\Big\{\frac{1}{2}{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\big(\Delta\Psi_{\sigma}+\nabla\mbox{div}\,\Psi_{\sigma}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\,\,\,\,\,+\Big(\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\big(\nabla\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\cdot\nabla\big)+\nabla\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\cdot\big(\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\nabla\big)\Big)\Psi_{\sigma}\Big\}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\Big\{V^{\varepsilon}\frac{\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{x}}{r^{2}}+\frac{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}{r}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}u^{\varepsilon}\Big(I_{n\times n}-\frac{\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{x}}{r^{2}}\Big)\Big\}:\nabla\Psi_{\sigma}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t.

Next, we consider the limit:
\sigma\rightarrow 0
in (
4.61
). First, we define

(4.62)

\displaystyle\varphi(t,r):=\int_{\partial B_{1}(\mathbf{0})}\boldsymbol{\omega}\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi}(t,r\boldsymbol{\omega})\,{\rm d}\boldsymbol{\omega}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r^{n-1}}\int_{\partial B_{r}(\mathbf{0})}\boldsymbol{\omega}\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi}(t,\mathbf{y})\,{\rm d}S_{\mathbf{y}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r^{n-1}}\int_{B_{r}(\mathbf{0})}\mbox{div}\,\boldsymbol{\psi}(t,\mathbf{y})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{y},

which implies that

(4.63)

\displaystyle|\varphi(t,r)|\leq C(\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{C^{1}})r;

also see
[
41
,
68
]
.
For the term involving the potential, we notice from (
4.30
) and (
4.63
) that

\displaystyle\Big|\rho^{\varepsilon}\,(\Phi_{r}^{\varepsilon}r^{n-1})\,\varphi\Big|\leq C(\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{C^{1}})\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)r\int_{0}^{r}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z

for
(t,r)\in[0,\infty)\times[0,\infty)
, which, together with Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem
and (
4.26
), yields that

(4.64)

\displaystyle\lim_{\sigma\rightarrow 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\rho^{\varepsilon}\nabla\Phi^{\varepsilon}\cdot\Psi_{\sigma}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\lim_{\sigma\rightarrow 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\rho^{\varepsilon}\Phi_{r}^{\varepsilon}\,\varphi\chi_{\sigma}(r)\,\omega_{n}r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\rho^{\varepsilon}\Phi_{r}^{\varepsilon}\,\varphi\,\omega_{n}r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\rho^{\varepsilon}\nabla\Phi^{\varepsilon}\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t.

Using (
4.62
), Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem, and Proposition
4.5
, we have

(4.65)

\displaystyle\lim_{\sigma\rightarrow 0}\Big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{t}\Psi_{\sigma}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}_{0}\cdot\Psi_{\sigma}(0,\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\textbf{x}\Big\}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\sigma\rightarrow 0}\Big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}m^{\varepsilon}\,\partial_{t}\varphi\,\chi_{\sigma}(r)\,\omega_{n}r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t+\int_{0}^{\infty}m_{0}^{\varepsilon}\,\varphi(0,r)\chi_{\sigma}(r)\,\omega_{n}r^{n-1}{\rm d}r\Big\}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}m^{\varepsilon}\,\partial_{t}\varphi\,\omega_{n}r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t+\int_{0}^{\infty}m_{0}^{\varepsilon}\,\varphi(0,r)\,\omega_{n}r^{n-1}{\rm d}r

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\psi}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}_{0}\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi}(0,\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}.

Employing (
4.63
) and Proposition
4.5
, we have

(4.66)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\big(\frac{|m^{\varepsilon}|^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}+p(\rho^{\varepsilon})\big)\,\boldsymbol{\psi}\cdot\frac{\mathbf{x}}{r}\,\chi^{\prime}_{\sigma}(r)\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\sigma}^{2\sigma}\big(\frac{|m^{\varepsilon}|^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}+p(\rho^{\varepsilon})\big)\big|\varphi(t,r)\chi^{\prime}_{\sigma}(r)\big|\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\sigma}^{2\sigma}\big(\frac{|m^{\varepsilon}|^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}+p(\rho^{\varepsilon})\big)\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\longrightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as $\sigma\rightarrow 0$},

(4.67)

\displaystyle\Big|\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\frac{m^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}})_{r}\boldsymbol{\psi}\cdot\frac{\mathbf{x}}{r}\chi^{\prime}_{\sigma}(r)\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\sigma}^{2\sigma}\big|\frac{m^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}})_{r}\varphi(t,r)\chi^{\prime}_{\sigma}(r)\big|\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\sigma}^{2\sigma}\big(\frac{|m^{\varepsilon}|^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}+\varepsilon^{2}|(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}})_{r}|^{2}\big)\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\longrightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as $\sigma\rightarrow 0$},

and

(4.68)

\displaystyle\Big|\sqrt{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\chi^{\prime}_{\sigma}(r)\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\Big\{V^{\varepsilon}\frac{\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{x}}{r^{2}}+\frac{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}{r}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}u^{\varepsilon}\Big(I_{n\times n}-\frac{\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{x}}{r^{2}}\Big)\Big\}:\Big(\boldsymbol{\psi}\otimes\frac{\mathbf{x}}{r}\Big)\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\,{\rm d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\quad=\Big|\sqrt{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\chi^{\prime}_{\sigma}(r)\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}V^{\varepsilon}\boldsymbol{\psi}\cdot\frac{\mathbf{x}}{r}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\Big|\sqrt{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\sigma}^{2\sigma}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}V^{\varepsilon}\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\Big|\longrightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as $\sigma\rightarrow 0$}.

Using (
4.66
)–(
4.68
), Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem, and Proposition
4.5
, we obtain

(4.69)

\displaystyle\lim_{\sigma\rightarrow 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\Big\{\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\big(\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\nabla\big)\Psi_{\sigma}+p(\rho^{\varepsilon})\,\mbox{div}\,\Psi_{\sigma}\Big\}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\Big\{\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\big(\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\nabla\big)\boldsymbol{\psi}+p(\rho^{\varepsilon})\,\mbox{div}\,\boldsymbol{\psi}\Big\}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t,

(4.70)

\displaystyle\lim_{\sigma\rightarrow 0}\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\Big\{\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\big(\nabla\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\cdot\nabla\big)\Psi_{\sigma}+\nabla\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\cdot\big(\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\nabla\big)\Psi_{\sigma}\Big\}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad=\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\Big\{\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\big(\nabla\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\cdot\nabla\big)\boldsymbol{\psi}+\nabla\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\cdot\big(\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\nabla\big)\boldsymbol{\psi}\Big\}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t,

(4.71)

\displaystyle\lim_{\sigma\rightarrow 0}\sqrt{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\Big\{V^{\varepsilon}\frac{\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{x}}{r^{2}}+\frac{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}{r}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}u^{\varepsilon}\big(I_{n\times n}-\frac{\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{x}}{r^{2}}\big)\Big\}:\nabla\Psi_{\sigma}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad=\sqrt{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\Big\{V^{\varepsilon}\frac{\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{x}}{r^{2}}+\frac{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}{r}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}u^{\varepsilon}\big(I_{n\times n}-\frac{\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{x}}{r^{2}}\big)\Big\}:\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t.

We notice that

(4.72)

\displaystyle\Delta(\Psi_{\sigma})_{i}=\chi_{\sigma}(r)\Delta\psi_{i}+2\nabla\psi_{i}\cdot\nabla\chi_{\sigma}(r)+\psi_{i}\Delta\chi_{\sigma}(r),

\displaystyle\partial_{i}(\mbox{div}\,\Psi_{\sigma})=\chi_{\sigma}(r)\partial_{i}(\mbox{div}\,\boldsymbol{\psi})+\mbox{div}\,\boldsymbol{\psi}\partial_{i}\chi_{\sigma}(r)+\partial_{i}\boldsymbol{\psi}\cdot\nabla\chi_{\sigma}(r)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\,+\frac{x_{i}}{r}\chi^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma}(r)\boldsymbol{\psi}\cdot\frac{\mathbf{x}}{r}+\chi^{\prime}_{\sigma}(r)\big(\boldsymbol{\psi}\cdot\frac{\nabla x_{i}}{r}-\boldsymbol{\psi}\cdot\frac{\mathbf{x}}{r}\frac{x_{i}}{r^{2}}\big).

It follows from (
4.63
) and Proposition
4.5
that

(4.73)

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}m^{\varepsilon}\,\frac{x_{i}}{r}\,\Big\{2\nabla\psi_{i}\cdot\nabla\chi_{\sigma}+\psi_{i}\Delta\chi_{\sigma}+\mbox{div}\,\boldsymbol{\psi}\,\partial_{i}\chi_{\sigma}(r)+\partial_{i}\boldsymbol{\psi}\cdot\nabla\chi_{\sigma}(r)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\,\,\,\,\left.+\frac{x_{i}}{r}\chi^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma}(r)\boldsymbol{\psi}\cdot\frac{\mathbf{x}}{r}+\chi^{\prime}_{\sigma}(r)\big(\boldsymbol{\psi}\cdot\frac{\nabla x_{i}}{r}-\boldsymbol{\psi}\cdot\frac{\mathbf{x}}{r}\frac{x_{i}}{r^{2}}\big)\Big\}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t\right|

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{C^{1}})\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\sigma}^{2\sigma}\varepsilon|m^{\varepsilon}|\Big(|\chi^{\prime}_{\sigma}(r)|+\frac{1}{r}\varphi(r)|\chi^{\prime}_{\sigma}(r)|+\varphi(r)|\chi^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma}(r)|\Big)\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{C^{1}})\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\sigma}^{2\sigma}\varepsilon|m^{\varepsilon}|\,r^{n-2}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{C^{1}})\left\{\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\sigma}^{2\sigma}\rho^{\varepsilon}\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\left\{\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\sigma}^{2\sigma}\frac{|m^{\varepsilon}|^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\,r^{n-3}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}\longrightarrow 0

as
\sigma\rightarrow 0
.
Thus, using (
4.72
)–(
4.73
), Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem, and Proposition
4.5
,
we have

(4.74)

\displaystyle\lim_{\sigma\rightarrow 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\big\{\Delta\Psi_{\sigma}+\nabla\mbox{div}\,\Psi_{\sigma}\big\}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\big\{\Delta\boldsymbol{\psi}+\nabla\mbox{div}\,\boldsymbol{\psi}\big\}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t.

Substituting (
4.64
)–(
4.65
), (
4.69
)–(
4.71
), and (
4.74
)
into (
4.61
) leads to (
4.53
).
∎

Lemma 4.11

Let
\xi(\mathbf{x})\in C_{0}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
be any smooth function with compact support.
Then

(4.75)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\nabla\Phi^{\varepsilon}(t,\mathbf{x})\cdot\nabla\xi(\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}=-\kappa\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,\mathbf{x})\xi(\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\qquad\mbox{for $t\geq 0$}.

The proof is direct by using
\eqref{5.5-2}_{3}
, (
4.30
), and (
4.45
), so we omit the details here.

4.2
H^{-1}_{\rm loc}
-Compactness

To use the compensated compactness framework in
[
8
]
,
we need the
H_{\rm loc}^{-1}
-compactness of entropy dissipation measures.

Lemma 4.12

(
H_{\rm loc}^{-1}
–compactness)

Let
(\eta,q)
be a weak entropy pair defined in (
2.11
)
for any smooth compact supported function
\psi(s)
on
\mathbb{R}
.
Then, for
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
,

(4.76)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\eta(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})+\partial_{r}q(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})\qquad\mbox{is compact in $H^{-1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$}.

Proof.

To obtain (
4.76
),
we have to make the argument in the weak sense, since
(\rho^{\varepsilon},\mathcal{M}^{\varepsilon},\Phi^{\varepsilon})
is a weak solution
of CNSPEs (
1.4
).
In fact, we first have to study the equation for
\partial_{t}\eta(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})+\partial_{r}q(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})
in
the distributional sense, which is more complicated than that in
[
8
,
9
]
.
We divide the proof into five steps.

1. Since

\displaystyle\eta(\rho,\rho u)

\displaystyle=\rho\int_{-1}^{1}\psi(u+\rho^{\theta}s)[1-s^{2}]_{+}^{\mathfrak{b}}\,{\rm d}s,

\displaystyle q(\rho,\rho u)

\displaystyle=\rho\int_{-1}^{1}(u+\theta\rho^{\theta}s)\psi(u+\rho^{\theta}s)[1-s^{2}]_{+}^{\mathfrak{b}}\,{\rm d}s,

then it follows from
[
8
, Lemma 2.1]
that

(4.77)

\displaystyle|\eta(\rho,\rho u)|+|q(\rho,\rho u)|\leq C_{\psi}\rho\qquad\mbox{for $\gamma\in(1,3]$},

(4.78)

\displaystyle|\eta(\rho,\rho u)|\leq C_{\psi}\rho,\quad|q(\rho,\rho u)|\leq C_{\psi}\big(\rho+\rho^{1+\theta}\big)\quad\mbox{for $\gamma\in(3,\infty)$},

(4.79)

\displaystyle|\partial_{\rho}\eta(\rho,\rho u)|\leq C_{\psi}\big(1+\rho^{\theta}\big),\quad|\partial_{m}\eta(\rho,\rho u)|\leq C_{\psi}.

On the other hand, if we regard
\partial_{m}\eta(\rho,m)
as a function of
(\rho,u)
, then

(4.80)

\displaystyle|\partial_{m\rho}\eta|\leq C_{\psi}\rho^{\theta-1},\qquad|\partial_{mu}\eta|\leq C_{\psi}.

2. Denote
(\eta^{\varepsilon,b},q^{\varepsilon,b}):=(\eta,q)(\rho^{\varepsilon,b},m^{\varepsilon,b})

and
(\eta^{\varepsilon},q^{\varepsilon}):=(\eta,q)(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})
for simplicity. Multiplying
\eqref{2.1}_{1}
by
\eta_{\rho}(\rho^{\varepsilon,b},m^{\varepsilon,b})
,
\eqref{2.1}_{2}
by
\eta_{m}(\rho^{\varepsilon,b},m^{\varepsilon,b})
, and add them together to obtain

(4.81)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\eta^{\varepsilon,b}+\partial_{r}q^{\varepsilon,b}

\displaystyle=-\frac{n-1}{r}m^{\varepsilon,b}\big(\eta_{\rho}^{\varepsilon,b}+u^{\varepsilon,b}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b}\big)-\kappa\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b}\frac{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}{r^{n-1}}\int_{0}^{r}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon\eta^{\varepsilon,b}_{m}\Big\{\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(u_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}+\frac{n-1}{r}u^{\varepsilon,b})\big)_{r}-\frac{n-1}{r}u^{\varepsilon,b}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{r}\Big\},

where
\rho^{\varepsilon,b}
is understood to be zero in domain
[0,T]\times[0,a)

so that
\int_{a}^{r}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z
can be rewritten
as
\int_{0}^{r}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z
in the potential term.

Let
\phi(t,r)\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
and
b\gg 1
so that
{\rm supp}\,\phi(t,\cdot)\Subset(a,b(t))
.
Then multiplying (
4.81
) by
\phi
and integrating by parts yield that

(4.82)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\big(\partial_{t}\eta^{\varepsilon,b}+\partial_{r}q^{\varepsilon,b}\big)\phi\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\frac{n-1}{r}m^{\varepsilon,b}\big(\eta_{\rho}^{\varepsilon,b}+u^{\varepsilon,b}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b}\big)\phi\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad-\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b})_{r}\,\Big(u_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}+\frac{n-1}{r}u^{\varepsilon,b}\Big)\phi\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad-\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b}\,\Big(u_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}+\frac{n-1}{r}u^{\varepsilon,b}\Big)\phi_{r}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t
