\displaystyle\quad-\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b}\frac{n-1}{r}u^{\varepsilon,b}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{r}\phi\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b}\frac{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}{r^{n-1}}\Big(\int_{0}^{r}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\Big)\phi\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle:=\sum_{i=1}^{5}I_{i}^{\varepsilon,b}.

3. It is direct to see that

(4.83)

\displaystyle\eta^{\varepsilon,b}\longrightarrow\eta^{\varepsilon}\qquad\mbox{{\it a.e.} in $\{(t,r)\,:\,\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)\neq 0\}\,$ as $b\rightarrow\infty$}.

In
\{(t,r)\,:\,\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0\}
, it follows from (
4.77
)–(
4.78
) that

(4.84)

\displaystyle|\eta^{\varepsilon,b}|\leq C_{\psi}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\longrightarrow 0=\eta^{\varepsilon}\qquad\,\mbox{as $b\rightarrow\infty$}.

Combining (
4.83
)–(
4.84
) together, we have

(4.85)

\displaystyle\eta^{\varepsilon,b}\longrightarrow\eta^{\varepsilon}\qquad\mbox{{\it a.e.} as $b\rightarrow\infty$}.

Similarly, we also have

(4.86)

\displaystyle q^{\varepsilon,b}\longrightarrow q^{\varepsilon}\qquad\mbox{{\it a.e.} as $b\rightarrow\infty$}.

Let
K\Subset(0,\infty)
be any compact subset. For
\gamma\in(1,3]
,
it follows from (
3.71
) and (
4.77
) that

(4.87)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\big(|\eta^{\varepsilon,b}|+|q^{\varepsilon,b}|\big)^{\gamma+1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{\psi}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}|^{\gamma+1}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{\psi}(K,M,E_{0},T).

For
\gamma\in(3,\infty)
, it follows from (
4.78
) and (
3.100
) that

(4.88)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\big(|\eta^{\varepsilon,b}|+|q^{\varepsilon,b}|\big)^{\frac{\gamma+\theta}{1+\theta}}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{\psi}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\big(|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}|^{\frac{\gamma+\theta}{1+\theta}}+|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}|^{\gamma+\theta}\big)\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{\psi}(K,M,E_{0},T).

We take
p_{1}=\gamma+1>2
when
\gamma\in(1,3]
, and
p_{1}=\frac{\gamma+\theta}{1+\theta}>2
when
\gamma\in(3,\infty)
.
Then it follows from (
4.87
)–(
4.88
) that

\displaystyle(\eta^{\varepsilon,b},\ q^{\varepsilon,b})\qquad\mbox{is uniformly bounded in }L^{p_{1}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}),

which, together with (
4.85
) and (
4.86
), yields that, up to a subsequence,

\displaystyle(\eta^{\varepsilon,b},\ q^{\varepsilon,b})\to(\eta^{\varepsilon},\ q^{\varepsilon})\qquad\mbox{in $L^{2}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$ as $b\rightarrow\infty$}.

Thus, for any
\phi\in C^{1}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
, we see that,
as
b\rightarrow\infty
(up to a subsequence),

(4.89)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\big(\partial_{t}\eta^{\varepsilon,b}+\partial_{r}q^{\varepsilon,b}\big)\phi\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\big(\eta^{\varepsilon,b}\partial_{t}\phi+q^{\varepsilon,b}\partial_{r}\phi\big)\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\longrightarrow-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\big(\eta^{\varepsilon}\partial_{t}\phi+q^{\varepsilon}\partial_{r}\phi\big)\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t.

Furthermore,

(\eta^{\varepsilon},q^{\varepsilon})
is uniformly bounded in
L^{p_{1}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
for some
p_{1}>2
,
which implies that

(4.90)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\eta^{\varepsilon}+\partial_{r}q^{\varepsilon}\qquad\mbox{is uniformly bounded in $\varepsilon>0$ in $W^{-1,p_{1}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})$}.

4. Now we estimate the terms of (
4.82
)-RHS.
For
I_{1}^{\varepsilon,b}
, a direct calculation shows that

|\eta_{\rho}+u\eta_{m}|\leq C_{\psi}(1+\rho^{\theta})

which,
together with Lemma
4.4
and similar arguments in (
4.83
)–(
4.85
), yields that

(4.91)

\displaystyle\frac{n-1}{r}m^{\varepsilon,b}\big(\eta_{\rho}^{\varepsilon,b}+u^{\varepsilon,b}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b}\big)\longrightarrow\frac{n-1}{r}m^{\varepsilon}\big(\eta_{\rho}^{\varepsilon}+u^{\varepsilon}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon}\big)\quad\mbox{{\it a.e.} as $b\rightarrow\infty$}.

Then it follows from
(
4.28
) that

(4.92)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\Big|\frac{n-1}{r}m^{\varepsilon,b}\big(\eta_{\rho}^{\varepsilon,b}+u^{\varepsilon,b}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b}\big)\Big|^{\frac{7}{6}}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}|u^{\varepsilon,b}|^{2}+\rho^{\varepsilon,b}+(\rho^{\varepsilon,b})^{\gamma}\big)^{\frac{7}{6}}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\displaystyle C\Big(1+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}|u^{\varepsilon,b}|^{3}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\Big)^{\frac{7}{9}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\big(1+|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}|^{\gamma+1}\big){\rm d}r{\rm d}t\Big)^{\frac{2}{9}}\,\,\,\mbox{for $\gamma\in(1,3]$},\\[8.53581pt]
\displaystyle C\Big(1+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}|u^{\varepsilon,b}|^{3}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\Big)^{\frac{7}{9}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\big(1+|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}|^{\gamma+\theta}\big){\rm d}r{\rm d}t\Big)^{\frac{2}{9}}\,\,\,\mbox{for $\gamma\in(3,\infty)$}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\leq C(K,M,E_{0},T).

Using (
4.91
)–(
4.92
), up to a subsequence, we have

(4.93)

\displaystyle I^{\varepsilon,b}_{1}\longrightarrow-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\frac{n-1}{r}m^{\varepsilon}\big(\eta_{\rho}^{\varepsilon}+u^{\varepsilon}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon}\big)\phi\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\qquad\mbox{as $b\rightarrow\infty$},

(4.94)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\Big|\frac{n-1}{r}m^{\varepsilon}\big(\eta_{\rho}^{\varepsilon}+u^{\varepsilon}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon}\big)\Big|^{\frac{7}{6}}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\leq C(K,M,E_{0},T).

For
I_{2}^{\varepsilon,b},I_{4}^{\varepsilon,b}
, and
I_{5}^{\varepsilon,b}
, it follows from Lemmas
3.1
and
3.3
, and (
4.79
)–(
4.80
) that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\Big|\varepsilon\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b})_{r}\,(u_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}+\frac{n-1}{r}u^{\varepsilon,b})\Big|\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad\leq C_{\psi}(K)\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\Big(\varepsilon\rho^{\varepsilon,b}|u_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}|^{2}+\varepsilon(\rho^{\varepsilon,b})^{\gamma-2}|\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}|^{2}+\rho^{\varepsilon,b}|u^{\varepsilon,b}|^{2}\Big)\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad\leq C_{\psi}(K,M,E_{0},T),

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\Big|\varepsilon\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b}\frac{n-1}{r}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{r}u^{\varepsilon,b}\Big|\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad\leq C_{\psi}(K)\Big(\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}|(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}})_{r}|^{2}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}|u^{\varepsilon,b}|^{2}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C_{\psi}(K,M,E_{0},T),

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\Big|\kappa\eta^{\varepsilon,b}_{m}\frac{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}{r^{n-1}}\int_{0}^{r}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\Big|{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\leq C_{\psi}(K,M)\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 187.78836pt\leq C_{\psi}(K,M,E_{0},T).

Thus, there exist local bounded Radon measures
\mu_{1}^{\varepsilon},\mu_{2}^{\varepsilon}
, and
\mu_{3}^{\varepsilon}
on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
so that,
as
b\to\infty
(up to a subsequence),

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b})_{r}\,(u_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}+\frac{n-1}{r}u^{\varepsilon,b})\rm-\!\!\!\rightharpoonup\mu_{1}^{\varepsilon},

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b}\frac{n-1}{r}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{r}u^{\varepsilon,b}\rm-\!\!\!\rightharpoonup\mu_{2}^{\varepsilon},

\displaystyle-\kappa\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b}\frac{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}{r^{n-1}}\int_{0}^{r}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\rm-\!\!\!\rightharpoonup\mu_{3}^{\varepsilon}.

In addition,

(4.95)

\mu_{i}^{\varepsilon}((0,T)\times\mathcal{O})\leq C_{\psi}(K,T,E_{0})\qquad\,\,\mbox{for $i=1,2,3$},

for each open subset
\mathcal{O}\subset K
.
Then, up to a subsequence, we have

(4.96)

\displaystyle I_{2}^{\varepsilon,b}+I_{4}^{\varepsilon,b}+I_{5}^{\varepsilon,b}\longrightarrow\langle\mu_{1}^{\varepsilon}+\mu_{2}^{\varepsilon}+\mu_{3}^{\varepsilon},\phi\rangle\qquad\mbox{as $b\rightarrow\infty$}.

For
I_{3}^{\varepsilon,b}
, we notice from Lemma
3.1
that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\Big|\sqrt{\varepsilon}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b}\,(u_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}+\frac{n-1}{r}u^{\varepsilon,b})\Big|^{\frac{4}{3}}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{\psi}(K)\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\Big|\sqrt{\varepsilon}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(|u_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}|+|u^{\varepsilon,b}|)\Big|^{\frac{4}{3}}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{\psi}(K)\Big(\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}|u_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}|^{2}+\rho^{\varepsilon,b}|u^{\varepsilon,b}|^{2}\big)\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\Big)^{\frac{2}{3}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}|^{2}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\psi}(K,M,E_{0},T).

Then there exists a function
f^{\varepsilon}
such that, as
b\to\infty
(up to a subsequence),

(4.97)

\displaystyle-\sqrt{\varepsilon}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b}\,\big(u_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}+\frac{n-1}{r}u^{\varepsilon,b}\big)\,{\rm-\!\!\!\rightharpoonup}\,f^{\varepsilon}\qquad\,\mbox{weakly in $L^{\frac{4}{3}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$},

(4.98)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}|f^{\varepsilon}|^{\frac{4}{3}}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\leq C_{\psi}(K,M,E_{0},T).

Thus, it follows from (
4.97
) that

(4.99)

\displaystyle I_{3}^{\varepsilon,b}\longrightarrow\sqrt{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}f^{\varepsilon}\phi_{r}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\qquad\mbox{as $b\to\infty$ (up to a subsequence)}.

5. Taking
b\rightarrow\infty
(up to a subsequence) on both sides of (
4.82
), then it follows
from (
4.89
), (
4.93
), (
4.96
), and (
4.99
) that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\eta^{\varepsilon}+\partial_{r}q^{\varepsilon}=-\frac{n-1}{r}\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon}\big(\eta_{\rho}^{\varepsilon}+u^{\varepsilon}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon}\big)+\mu_{1}^{\varepsilon}+\mu_{2}^{\varepsilon}+\mu_{3}^{\varepsilon}-\sqrt{\varepsilon}f^{\varepsilon}_{r}

in the sense of distributions. Noting (
4.94
)–(
4.95
), we know that

(4.100)

\displaystyle-\frac{n-1}{r}\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon}\big(\eta_{\rho}^{\varepsilon}+u^{\varepsilon}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon}\big)+\mu_{1}^{\varepsilon}+\mu_{2}^{\varepsilon}+\mu_{3}^{\varepsilon}\,\,\,\mbox{is a local bounded Radon measure},

and the bound is uniform in
\varepsilon>0
.
From (
4.98
), we know that

(4.101)

\displaystyle\sqrt{\varepsilon}f^{\varepsilon}_{r}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{in $W^{-1,\frac{4}{3}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$ as $\varepsilon\rightarrow 0+$}.

Then it follows from (
4.100
)–(
4.101
) that

(4.102)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\eta^{\varepsilon}+\partial_{r}q^{\varepsilon}\,\,\,\mbox{are confined in a compact subset of $W^{-1,p_{2}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$}

for some
p_{2}\in(1,2)
.

The interpolation compactness theorem (
cf
.
[
6
,
16
]
) indicates that,
for
p_{2}>1
,
p_{1}\in(p_{2},\infty]
, and
p_{0}\in[p_{2},p_{1})
,

\displaystyle(\mbox{compact set of}\ W^{-1,p_{2}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}))\cap(\mbox{bounded set of}\ W^{-1,p_{1}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}))

\displaystyle\subset(\mbox{compact set of}\ W^{-1,p_{0}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})),

which is a generalization of Murat’s lemma in
[
64
,
71
]
.
Combining this
theorem for
1<p_{2}<2
,
p_{1}>2
, and
p_{0}=2
with the facts in (
4.90
) and (
4.102
), we conclude (
4.76
).
∎

Remark 4.13
.

Since
(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})
are the weak solutions of CNSPEs, it is not convenient to use the weak formulation to
prove the
H_{\rm loc}^{-1}
-compactness directly.
Therefore, in this section above, we first study the equation satisfied by
\partial_{t}\eta^{\varepsilon,b}+\partial_{r}q^{\varepsilon,b}
,
then take limit
b\rightarrow\infty
to obtain the equation satisfied by
\partial_{t}\eta^{\varepsilon}+\partial_{r}q^{\varepsilon}
in the distributional sense,
and finally use the equation to establish the
H_{\rm loc}^{-1}
–compactness.

Combining Proposition
4.5
with Lemmas
4.6
and
4.8
–
4.12
, we have

Theorem 4.14

Let
(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon},m_{0}^{\varepsilon})
be the initial data satisfying (
2.13
)–(
2.18
).
Then, for each
\varepsilon>0
, there exists a global spherically symmetric weak
solution

(\rho^{\varepsilon},{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon},\Phi^{\varepsilon})(t,\mathbf{x}):=(\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r),m^{\varepsilon}(t,r)\frac{\mathbf{x}}{r},\Phi^{\varepsilon}(t,r))

of CNSPEs (
1.4
) in the sense of Definition
2.6
.
Moreover,
m^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)u^{\varepsilon}(t,r)
, with

u^{\varepsilon}(t,r):=\frac{m^{\varepsilon}(t,r)}{\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)}\,
a.e. on
\{(t,r)\,:\,\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)\neq 0\}

and
u^{\varepsilon}(t,r):=0\,
a.e. on
\{(t,r)\,:\,\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0\,\,\mbox{or $\,r=0$}\}
,
satisfies the following properties
:

(4.103)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\hskip 14.22636pt\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)\geq 0\quad a.e.,\end{split}

(4.104)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636ptu^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0,\,\,\big(\frac{m^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\big)(t,r)=\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}(t,r)u^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pta.e.\,\,\mbox{on $\{(t,r)\,:\,\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0\}$},

(4.105)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\hskip 14.22636pt\Phi_{r}^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=\frac{\kappa}{r^{n-1}}\int_{0}^{r}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\qquad\,\,\mbox{for}\,\,(t,r)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2},\\
\end{split}

(4.106)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\hskip 14.22636pt\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r=\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}\qquad\,\,\mbox{for all $t\geq 0$},\end{split}

(4.107)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(\frac{1}{2}\rho^{\varepsilon}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}+(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma}\big)(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}(\rho^{\varepsilon}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{2})(s,r)\,r^{n-3}{\rm d}r{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{\infty}|\Phi_{r}^{\varepsilon}(t,r)|^{2}\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r+\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int_{0}^{r}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\Big)\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)\,r{\rm d}r

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(M,E_{0})\qquad\,\,\mbox{for all $t\geq 0$},

(4.108)

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{n-1}\big|\big(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)}\big)_{r}\big|^{2}{\rm d}r+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{n-1}\big|\big((\rho^{\varepsilon}(s,r))^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\big)_{r}\big|^{2}{\rm d}r{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(M,E_{0},T)\qquad\,\,\mbox{for $t\in[0,T]$},

(4.109)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{3}+(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma+\theta}+(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma+1}\big)(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(d,D,M,E_{0},T),

for any fixed
T>0
and any compact subset
[d,D]\Subset(0,\infty)
. Moreover, the following energy inequality holds
:

(4.110)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\Big|^{2}+\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})-\frac{\kappa}{2}|\nabla\Phi^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\Big)(t,\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}}}\Big|^{2}+\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}e(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon})-\frac{\kappa}{2}|\nabla\Phi_{0}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\Big)(\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\qquad\mbox{for $t\geq 0$}.

Furthermore, let
(\eta^{\psi},q^{\psi})
be an entropy pair defined in (
2.11
)
for a smooth function
\psi(s)
with compact support on
\mathbb{R}
.
Then, for
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\eta^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})+\partial_{r}q^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})\qquad\mbox{is compact in $H^{-1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$}.

5
Proof of the Main Theorems

In this section, we give a complete proof of Main Theorem II: Theorem
2.7
,
which leads to Main Theorem I: Theorem
2.2
, as indicated in Remark
2.8
.
We divide the proof into several steps.

1. The uniform estimates and compactness properties obtained in Theorem
4.14
imply that
the weak solutions

\displaystyle(\rho^{\varepsilon},{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon},\Phi^{\varepsilon})=(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon}\frac{\mathbf{x}}{r},\Phi^{\varepsilon})

of CNSPEs (
1.4
)
satisfy the compensated compactness framework in

[
8
]
.
Then the compactness theorem in
[
8
]
for the whole range
\gamma>1

implies that there exists a vector function
(\rho,m)(t,r)
such that

(5.1)

\displaystyle(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})\longrightarrow(\rho,m)\quad\mbox{{\it a.e.} $\,(t,r)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\,$ as $\varepsilon\rightarrow 0+$ (up to a subsequence)}.

By similar arguments as in the proof of Lemma
4.4
, we find that

m(t,r)=0

a.e.
on
\{(t,r)\,:\,\rho(t,r)=0\}
.
We can define the limit velocity
u(t,r)
by setting
u(t,r):=\frac{m(t,r)}{\rho(t,r)}

a.e.
on
\{(t,r)\,:\,\rho(t,r)\neq 0\}

and
u(t,r):=0

a.e.
on
\{(t,r)\,:\rho(t,r)=0\,\,\,\mbox{or $r=0$}\}
.
Then we have

\displaystyle m(t,r)=\rho(t,r)u(t,r).

We can also define
(\frac{m}{\sqrt{\rho}})(t,r):=\sqrt{\rho(t,r)}u(t,r)
, which is
0

a.e.
on
\{(t,r)\ :\ \rho(t,r)=0\}
.
Moreover, we obtain that, as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0+
,

\frac{m^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\equiv\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}u^{\varepsilon}\longrightarrow\frac{m}{\sqrt{\rho}}\equiv\sqrt{\rho}u\quad\mbox{strongly in $L^{2}([0,T]\times[d,D],r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t)$}

for any given
T
and
[d,D]\Subset(0,\infty)
.

Notice that
|m|^{\frac{3(\gamma+1)}{\gamma+3}}\leq C\big(\rho|u|^{3}+\rho^{\gamma+1}\big)

which, together with
(
4.109
), yields that

(5.2)

\displaystyle(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})\longrightarrow(\rho,m)\qquad\mbox{in $L^{p_{1}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})\times L^{p_{2}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$}

for
p_{1}\in[1,\gamma+1)
and
p_{2}\in[1,\frac{3(\gamma+1)}{\gamma+3})
,
where
L^{p_{j}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
represents
L^{p_{j}}([0,T]\times K)
for any
T>0
and
K\Subset(0,\infty)
,
j=1,2
.

From the same estimates, we also obtain the convergence of the mechanical energy as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0+:

\displaystyle\eta^{\ast}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})\longrightarrow\eta^{\ast}(\rho,m)\qquad\mbox{in $L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})$}.

Since
\eta^{\ast}(\rho,m)
is a convex function, by passing limit in (
4.23
) and (
4.25
), we have

(5.3)

\displaystyle\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big\{\eta^{\ast}(\rho,m)(t,r)+\rho(t,r)\big\}\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\leq C(M,E_{0})(t_{2}-t_{1}),

which indicates that

(5.4)

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big\{\eta^{\ast}(\rho,m)(t,r)+\rho(t,r)\big\}\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r\leq C(M,E_{0}).

That is,
\rho(t,r)\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R};r^{n-1}{\rm d}r))
, since
\eta^{\ast}(\rho,m)
contains a term:
\rho^{\gamma}
.
This indicates that
\rho(t,\mathbf{x})
is a function in
L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
for
\gamma>1
(rather than a measure in space-time),
so that no delta measure (
i.e.
, concentration) is formed in the density
\rho
in the time interval
[0,T]
, especially
at the origin
r=0
.

2. A direct calculation shows that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0+}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{D}\left|\int_{0}^{r}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z-\int_{0}^{r}\rho(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\right|{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq D\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0+}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\sigma}^{D}|\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,z)-\rho(t,z)|\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z{\rm d}t+C(D,T,M,E_{0})\sigma^{n(1-\frac{1}{\gamma})}

\displaystyle\leq C(D,T,M,E_{0})\sigma^{n(1-\frac{1}{\gamma})},

which, as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0+
(up to a subsequence), yields that

(5.5)

\displaystyle\Phi_{r}^{\varepsilon}(t,r)r^{n-1}

\displaystyle=\kappa\int_{0}^{r}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\kappa\int_{0}^{r}\rho(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\quad\mbox{\it a.e. $(t,r)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}$}.

Then, using Fatou’s lemma, (
4.23
), (
5.1
), (
5.5
), and similar arguments as in (
5.3
)–(
5.4
),
we have

(5.6)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int_{0}^{r}\rho(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\Big)\rho(t,r)\,r{\rm d}r\leq C(M,E_{0})\quad\mbox{for {\it a.e.} $t\geq 0$}.

Estimate (
4.31
) implies that there exists a function
\Phi(t,\mathbf{x})=\Phi(t,r)
such that, as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0+
(up to a subsequence),

(5.7)

\displaystyle\Phi^{\varepsilon}\rm-\!\!\!\rightharpoonup\Phi\,\,\,\mbox{{weak-}$\ast$ in $L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))$ and weakly in $L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))$},

(5.8)

\displaystyle\|\Phi(t)\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{n-2}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|\nabla\Phi(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C(M,E_{0})\qquad\mbox{{\it a.e.} $t\geq 0$}.

It follows from the uniqueness of limit and (
5.5
) that

(5.9)

\displaystyle\Phi_{r}(t,r)r^{n-1}=\kappa\int_{0}^{r}\rho(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\qquad\mbox{{\it a.e.} $(t,r)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}$}.

To prove the energy inequality for the case that
\kappa=1
(gaseous stars), we need the strong convergence of the potential functions.
Notice that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{n-1}}\Big|\int_{0}^{r}(\rho^{\varepsilon}-\rho)(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\Big|^{2}\leq C(M)r^{-n+1}\qquad\mbox{for $r>0$ and {\it a.e.} $t\geq 0$},

which yields that

(5.10)

\displaystyle\int_{k}^{\infty}\frac{1}{r^{n-1}}\Big|\int_{0}^{r}(\rho^{\varepsilon}-\rho)(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\Big|^{2}{\rm d}r\leq C(M)k^{-n+2}\qquad\mbox{{\it a.e.} $t\geq 0$}.

Using the Hölder inequality,

(5.11)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{n-1}}\Big|\int_{0}^{r}(\rho^{\varepsilon}-\rho)(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{-n+1}\left(\int_{0}^{r}\big((\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma}+\rho^{\gamma}\big)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\right)^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}\,r^{2n(1-\frac{1}{\gamma})}

\displaystyle\leq C(E_{0},M)\,r^{n+1-\frac{2n}{\gamma}}\qquad\mbox{{\it a.e.} $t\geq 0$}.

Since
n+1-\frac{2n}{\gamma}>-1
for
\gamma>\frac{2n}{n+2}
, then it follows from (
5.1
), (
5.11
),
and Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem that, for any given
k>0
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{k}\frac{1}{r^{n-1}}\Big|\int_{0}^{r}(\rho^{\varepsilon}-\rho)(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\Big|^{2}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\to 0\qquad\mbox{as $\varepsilon\to 0+$},

which, together with (
5.10
), yields that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0+}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}|(\Phi_{r}^{\varepsilon}-\Phi_{r})(t,r)|^{2}\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0+}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{r^{n-1}}\Big|\int_{0}^{r}(\rho^{\varepsilon}-\rho)(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\Big|^{2}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(M)Tk^{-n+2}+\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0+}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{k}\frac{1}{r^{n-1}}\Big|\int_{0}^{r}(\rho^{\varepsilon}-\rho)(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\Big|^{2}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(M,T)\,k^{-n+2}.

Then (
4.32
) follows by taking
k\to\infty
to obtain

(5.12)

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0+}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}|(\Phi_{r}^{\varepsilon}-\Phi_{r})(t,r)|^{2}\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t=0\qquad\mbox{if $\gamma>\frac{2n}{n+2}$}.

3. Now we define

\displaystyle(\rho,{\mathcal{M}},\Phi)(t,\mathbf{x})=(\rho(t,r),m(t,r)\frac{\mathbf{x}}{r},\Phi(t,r)).

Using (
4.110
), Fatou’s lemma, and (
5.12
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{{\mathcal{M}}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\Big|^{2}+\rho e(\rho)-\frac{\kappa}{2}|\nabla\Phi|^{2}\Big)(t,\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq(t_{2}-t_{1})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{{\mathcal{M}}_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}}\Big|^{2}+\rho_{0}e(\rho_{0})-\frac{\kappa}{2}|\nabla\Phi_{0}|^{2}\Big)(\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x},

which implies that, for
a.e.

t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{{\mathcal{M}}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\Big|^{2}+\rho e(\rho)-\frac{\kappa}{2}|\nabla\Phi|^{2}\Big)(t,\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{{\mathcal{M}}_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}}\Big|^{2}+\rho_{0}e(\rho_{0})-\frac{\kappa}{2}|\nabla\Phi_{0}|^{2}\Big)(\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}.

This leads to (
2.3
)–(
2.4
).

4. In the following, we prove that
(\rho,{\mathcal{M}},\Phi)(t,\mathbf{x})
is indeed a global weak solution of
problem (
1.1
)–(
1.3
) in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Let
\zeta(t,\mathbf{x})\in C_{0}^{1}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
be a smooth function with compact support.
Then it follows from (
4.38
) that

(5.13)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}\zeta_{t}+{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\zeta\big)\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})\zeta(0,\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}=0.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
{\rm supp}\,\zeta\subset[-T,T]\times B_{D}(\mathbf{0})
for some
T,D>0
.
Let
\phi(t,r)
be the corresponding function defined in (
4.42
).
Using (
4.46
), (
5.2
), and similar arguments as in the proof of Lemma
4.9
,
we see that, for any fixed
\sigma>0
,

(5.14)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0+}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{\sigma}(\mathbf{0})}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}\zeta_{t}+{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\zeta\big)\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0+}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\sigma}^{\infty}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}\phi_{t}+m^{\varepsilon}\phi_{r}\big)\,\omega_{n}r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\sigma}^{\infty}\big(\rho\phi_{t}+m\phi_{r}\big)\,\omega_{n}r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{\sigma}(\mathbf{0})}\big(\rho\zeta_{t}+{\mathcal{M}}\cdot\nabla\zeta\big)\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t.

Using (
5.4
) and similar arguments as in (
4.44
), we have

(5.15)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{B_{\sigma}(\mathbf{0})}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}-\rho\big)\zeta_{t}\;{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\zeta\|_{C^{1}},T)\Big\{\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\sigma}\big((\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma}+\rho^{\gamma}\big)\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\Big\}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\,\Big\{\int_{0}^{\sigma}r^{n-1}{\rm d}r\Big\}^{1-\frac{1}{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq C(E_{0},M,\|\zeta\|_{C^{1}},T)\,\sigma^{n(1-\frac{1}{\gamma})}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as $\sigma\rightarrow 0$},

and

(5.16)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B_{\sigma}(\mathbf{0})}\big({\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}-{\mathcal{M}}\big)\cdot\nabla\zeta\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\Big\{\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\sigma}\Big(\frac{|m^{\varepsilon}|^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}+\frac{m^{2}}{\rho}\Big)(t,r)|\phi_{r}|\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\Big\}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Big\{\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\sigma}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}+\rho\big)(t,r)\,|\phi_{r}|r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\Big\}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(E_{0},M,\|\zeta\|_{C^{1}},T)\sigma^{\frac{n}{2}(1-\frac{1}{\gamma})}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as $\sigma\rightarrow 0$},

which, together with (
5.14
)–(
5.16
), yields that

(5.17)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0+}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}\zeta_{t}+{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\zeta\big)\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\big(\rho\zeta_{t}+{\mathcal{M}}\cdot\nabla\zeta\big)\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t.

Taking
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0+
in (
5.13
) and using (
5.17
), we conclude that
(\rho,{\mathcal{M}})
satisfies (
2.5
).

Next, we consider the momentum equation.
Let
\boldsymbol{\psi}=(\psi_{1},\cdots,\psi_{n})\in\big(C_{0}^{2}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{n})\big)^{n}
be a smooth function with compact support,
and let
\chi_{\sigma}(r)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
be a cut-off function satisfying (
4.54
).
Without loss of generality, we assume that
{\rm supp}\,\boldsymbol{\psi}\subset[-T,T]\times B_{D}(\mathbf{0})
for some
T,D>0
.
Denote
\Psi_{\sigma}=\boldsymbol{\psi}\chi_{\sigma}
. Then we have

(5.18)

\displaystyle\Big|\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}\Big\{\frac{1}{2}{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\big(\Delta\Psi_{\sigma}+\nabla\mbox{\rm div}\,\Psi_{\sigma}\big)+\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\big(\nabla\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\cdot\nabla\big)\Psi_{\sigma}

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt+\nabla\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\cdot\big(\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\nabla\big)\Psi_{\sigma}\Big\}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\sqrt{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\Big\{V^{\varepsilon}\frac{\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{x}}{r^{2}}+\frac{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}{r}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}u^{\varepsilon}\Big(I_{n\times n}-\frac{\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{x}}{r^{2}}\Big)\Big\}:\nabla\Psi_{\sigma}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\Big\{\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|V^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t+\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\frac{|m^{\varepsilon}|^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}r^{n-3}\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\Big\}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\{\varepsilon\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho^{\varepsilon}|\nabla\Psi_{\sigma}|^{2}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t\Big\}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\sigma,E_{0},D,T)\sqrt{\varepsilon}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as $\varepsilon\rightarrow 0+$}.

For the potential term, it follows from (
5.2
) and (
5.7
) that

(5.19)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0+}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\rho^{\varepsilon}\nabla\Phi^{\varepsilon}\cdot\Psi_{\sigma}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\rho\nabla\Phi\cdot\Psi_{\sigma}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t.

Using (
5.18
)–(
5.19
) and passing limit
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0+
(up to a subsequence) in (
4.61
) yield that

(5.20)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\Big\{{\mathcal{M}}\cdot\partial_{t}\Psi_{\sigma}+\frac{{\mathcal{M}}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\cdot\big(\frac{{\mathcal{M}}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\cdot\nabla\big)\Psi_{\sigma}+p(\rho)\,\mbox{div}\,\Psi_{\sigma}-\rho\nabla\Phi\cdot\Psi_{\sigma}\Big\}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}{\mathcal{M}}_{0}\cdot\Psi_{\sigma}(0,\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}=0.

In the following, we take limit
\sigma\rightarrow 0
in (
5.20
).
Notice that, for any
T>0
and
D>0
,

(5.21)

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{D}\big(\frac{m^{2}}{\rho}+p(\rho)\big)(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\leq C(E_{0},M,D,T),

which, together with similar arguments as in (
4.65
), yields that

(5.22)

\displaystyle\lim_{\sigma\rightarrow 0+}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}{\mathcal{M}}\cdot\partial_{t}\Psi_{\sigma}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}{\mathcal{M}}_{0}\cdot\Psi_{\sigma}(0,\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}{\mathcal{M}}\cdot\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\psi}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}{\mathcal{M}}_{0}\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi}(0,\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}.

Using (
4.62
)–(
4.63
) and (
5.21
), we have

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\big(\frac{m^{2}}{\rho}+p(\rho)\big)\,\boldsymbol{\psi}\cdot\frac{\mathbf{x}}{r}\,\chi^{\prime}_{\sigma}(r)\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\sigma}^{2\sigma}\big(\frac{m^{2}}{\rho}+p(\rho)\big)\,|\varphi(t,r)\chi^{\prime}_{\sigma}(r)|\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\sigma}^{2\sigma}\big(\frac{m^{2}}{\rho}+p(\rho)\big)\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t\longrightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as $\sigma\rightarrow 0+$},

which, together with (
5.21
) and Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem, yields that

(5.23)

\displaystyle\lim_{\sigma\rightarrow 0+}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\bigg\{\frac{{\mathcal{M}}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\cdot\big(\frac{{\mathcal{M}}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\cdot\nabla\big)\Psi_{\sigma}+p(\rho)\,\mbox{div}\,\Psi_{\sigma}\bigg\}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\bigg\{\frac{{\mathcal{M}}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\cdot\big(\frac{{\mathcal{M}}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\cdot\nabla\big)\boldsymbol{\psi}+p(\rho)\,\mbox{div}\,\boldsymbol{\psi}\bigg\}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t.

For the term involving the potential, using (
4.62
)–(
4.63
), we see that

\displaystyle\Big|\rho(t,r)\big(\int_{0}^{r}\rho(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\big)\,\varphi(t,r)\chi_{\sigma}(r)\Big|\leq C(\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{C^{1}})\,\rho(t,r)r\int_{0}^{r}\rho(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z,

which, together with (
5.6
), (
5.9
), and Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem, yields that

(5.24)

\displaystyle\lim_{\sigma\rightarrow 0+}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\rho\nabla\Phi\cdot\Psi_{\sigma}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\lim_{\sigma\rightarrow 0+}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\rho\Phi_{r}\,\varphi(t,r)\chi_{\sigma}(r)\,\omega_{n}r^{n-1}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\kappa\lim_{\sigma\rightarrow 0+}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\rho(t,r)\big(\int_{0}^{r}\rho(t,z)\,\omega_{n}z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\big)\varphi(t,r)\chi_{\sigma}(r)\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\kappa\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\rho(t,r)\big(\int_{0}^{r}\rho(t,z)\,\omega_{n}z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\big)\varphi(t,r)\,{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\rho\nabla\Phi\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}t.

Substituting (
5.22
)–(
5.24
)
into (
5.20
), we conclude that
(\rho,{\mathcal{M}},\Phi)
satisfies (
2.6
). By the Lebesgue theorem,
we can weaken the assumption that
\boldsymbol{\psi}\in(C_{0}^{2})^{n}
as
\boldsymbol{\psi}\in(C_{0}^{1})^{n}
.

Finally, we consider the Poisson equation.
Let
\xi(\mathbf{x})\in C_{0}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
be any smooth function with compact support.
For any
t_{2}>t_{1}\geq 0
, we use (
4.75
), (
5.7
), and similar arguments as in (
5.17
),
and then pass limit
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0+
(up to a subsequence) to obtain

(5.25)

\displaystyle-\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\nabla\Phi(s,\mathbf{x})\cdot\nabla\xi(\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}s=\kappa\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho(s,\mathbf{x})\xi(\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}s.

Applying the Lebesgue point theorem, we obtain that, for
a.e.

t\geq 0
,

(5.26)

\displaystyle\lim_{t_{2},t_{1}\rightarrow t}\frac{1}{t_{2}-t_{1}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\nabla\Phi(s,\mathbf{x})\cdot\nabla\xi(\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}s=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\nabla\Phi(t,\mathbf{x})\cdot\nabla\xi(\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x},

(5.27)

\displaystyle\lim_{t_{2},t_{1}\rightarrow t}\frac{1}{t_{2}-t_{1}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho(s,\mathbf{x})\xi(\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}{\rm d}s=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho(t,\mathbf{x})\xi(\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}.

Combining (
5.25
)–(
5.27
) together, we conclude that
(\rho,{\mathcal{M}},\Phi)
satisfies (
2.7
).

\hfill\Box

Appendix A
Sobolev’s Inequality and Construction of the Approximate Initial Data Sequences

In this appendix, we first state Sobolev’s inequality used in §3–§4; see
e.g.

[
49
, §8.3]
.

Lemma A.1

(Sobolev’s Inequality)

For
n\geq 3
, let
\nabla f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\lim_{|\mathbf{x}|\rightarrow\infty}f(\mathbf{x})=0
.
Then

(A.1)

\|f\|^{2}_{L^{\frac{2n}{n-2}}}\leq A_{n}\|\nabla f\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

where
A_{n}
is the best constant which is given by

(A.2)

A_{n}=\frac{4}{n(n-2)}\omega_{n+1}^{-\frac{2}{n}}

with
\omega_{n+1}=\frac{2\pi^{\frac{n+1}{2}}}{\Gamma(\frac{n+1}{2})}
as the surface area of unit sphere in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
.

We now construct the approximate initial data sequences
(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0},m_{0}^{\varepsilon})
and
(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0},u^{\varepsilon,b}_{0})

with desired estimates, regularity, and boundary compatibility.

To keep the
L^{p}
–properties of mollification, it is more convenient to smooth out the initial data
in the original coordinates in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
;
so we do not distinguish functions
(\rho_{0},m_{0})(r)
from
(\rho_{0},m_{0})(\mathbf{x})=(\rho_{0},m_{0})(|\mathbf{x}|)
for simplicity below.

For the initial data
(\rho_{0},m_{0})
, we assume that

(A.3)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big(\rho_{0}+\rho_{0}^{\gamma}+\rho_{0}^{\frac{2n}{n+2}}+\Big|\frac{m_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}}\Big|^{2}\Big)\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}<\infty\quad\mbox{for $\kappa=-1$ (plasmas) with $\gamma>1$},\\
&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big(\rho_{0}+\rho_{0}^{\gamma}+\Big|\frac{m_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}}\Big|^{2}\Big)\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}<\infty\qquad\qquad\mbox{for $\kappa=1$ (gaseous stars) with $\gamma>\frac{2n}{n+2}$},\end{split}

and denote

\Delta\Phi_{0}=\kappa\rho_{0},

which is well-defined, under assumption (
A.3
).

From now on, we denote
C>0
is a universal constant independent of
\varepsilon,\delta
, and
b
.

Let
J(\mathbf{x})
be the standard mollification function and

\displaystyle J_{\delta}(\textbf{x}):=\frac{1}{\delta^{n}}J(\frac{\mathbf{x}}{\delta})
for
\delta\in(0,1)
.
For later use, we take
\delta=\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}
and define
\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})
as

(A.4)

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x}):=\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\sqrt{\rho_{0}(\mathbf{x-y})}J_{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}(\mathbf{y})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{y}+\varepsilon e^{-|\mathbf{x}|^{2}}\Big)^{2}.

Then
\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})
is still a spherically symmetric function,

i.e.
,
\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})=\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon}(|\mathbf{x}|)
.
It is also direct to know that
\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})\geq\varepsilon^{2}e^{-2|\mathbf{x}|^{2}}>0
.

Lemma A.2

Let
q\in\{1,\gamma\}
for
\kappa=1

(
gaseous stars
)
, and
q\in\{1,\gamma,\frac{2n}{n+2}\}
for
\kappa=-1

(
plasmas
)
. Then

(A.5)

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{q}}\leq\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}}+C\varepsilon\qquad\mbox{as $\varepsilon\in(0,1]$},

(A.6)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0+}\big\{\|\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon}-\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}}+\|\sqrt{\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon}}-\sqrt{\rho_{0}}\|_{L^{2q}}\big\}=0,

(A.7)

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big|\nabla_{\mathbf{x}}\sqrt{\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\mathbf{x})}\Big|^{2}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\leq C\varepsilon\big(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{1}}+1\big)\longrightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as $\varepsilon\rightarrow 0+$}.

Proof.

It is direct to see that (
A.5
)–(
A.6
) follow
from the standard property of mollifier operator.
For (
A.7
), we notice that

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big|\nabla_{\mathbf{x}}\sqrt{\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\mathbf{x})}\Big|^{2}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\sqrt{\rho_{0}(\mathbf{x-y})}\nabla_{\mathbf{y}}J_{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}(\mathbf{y}){\rm d}\mathbf{y}\right|^{2}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}+C\varepsilon^{4}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{1}}\varepsilon+C\varepsilon^{4}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\,\,\,\,\mbox{as $\varepsilon\rightarrow 0+$}.

∎

In general, since
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\neq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho_{0}(\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}=M
, we define

(A.8)

\displaystyle\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x}):=\frac{M}{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}}\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x}).

Combining Lemma
A.2
and (
A.8
), we have

Lemma A.3

Let
q=1
and
\gamma>1
for
\kappa=1

(
gaseous stars
)
, and
q=1,\gamma
, and
\frac{2n}{n+2}
for
\kappa=-1

(
plasmas
)
.
Then

(A.9)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}=M,\qquad\|\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{q}}\leq C\big(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{p}}+1\big)\qquad\mbox{for all $\varepsilon\in(0,1]$},

(A.10)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0+}\big\{\|\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}-\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}}+\|\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}}-\sqrt{\rho_{0}}\|_{L^{2q}}\big\}=0,

(A.11)

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big|\nabla_{\mathbf{x}}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\mathbf{x})}\Big|^{2}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\leq C\varepsilon\big(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{1}}+1\big)\longrightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as $\varepsilon\rightarrow 0+$}.

We define

(A.12)

\displaystyle\Delta\Phi_{0}^{\varepsilon}=\kappa\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}.

Then a direct calculation yields that

Lemma A.4

\Phi_{0}^{\varepsilon}
satisfy

(A.13)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\mathbf{x}}(\Phi_{0}^{\varepsilon}-\Phi_{0})\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}-\rho_{0}\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{n+2}}}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as $\varepsilon\rightarrow 0+$}.

From (
A.4
), we know that
\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})
is a good approximation.
However, we don’t know yet whether

(A.14)

\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(b)\cong b^{-n+\alpha}\qquad\mbox{with $\alpha:=\min\{\frac{1}{2},(1-\frac{1}{\gamma})n\}$}

is satisfied. In fact, (
A.14
) (which agrees with condition (
3.12
)) is required
in the proof of Lemmas
3.3
–
3.4
.
To solve this problem, we denote
S=S(z)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
to be a cut-off function satisfying

(A.15)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}S(z)=0\quad\mbox{if $z\in(-\infty,0]$},\\
S(z)=1\quad\mbox{if $z\in[1,\infty)$},\\
S(z)\quad\mbox{is monotonic increasing in $[0,1]$}.\end{cases}

Now we define
\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(r)
by

(A.16)

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle:=\Big\{\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})}\,\big\{1-S(2(|\mathbf{x}|-(b-1)))\big\}+b^{-\frac{n-\alpha}{2}}\,S(2(|\mathbf{x}|-(b-1)))\Big\}^{2}.

It is direct to check that
\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(b)=b^{-(n-\alpha)}
,
which clearly satisfies (
A.14
).

Lemma A.5

The smooth functions
\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})
defined in (
A.16
) satisfy (
3.12
) and

(A.17)

\displaystyle\int_{|\mathbf{x}|\leq b}\Big(|\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})-\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})|^{q}+\Big|\sqrt{\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})}-\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})}\Big|^{2q}\Big)\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\rightarrow 0\,\,\,\mbox{as $b\to\infty$},

(A.18)

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\int_{|\mathbf{x}|\leq b}\Big|\nabla_{\mathbf{x}}\sqrt{\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})}\Big|^{2}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\leq C\big(1+\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{1}}\big)\,\varepsilon,

where
q\in\{1,\gamma\}
for
\kappa=1

(
gaseous stars
)
, and
q\in\{1,\gamma,\frac{2n}{n+2}\}
for
\kappa=-1

(
plasmas
)
.

Proof.

Using (
A.16
), a direct calculation shows that

(A.19)

\displaystyle\int_{|\mathbf{x}|\leq b}|\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})|^{q}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{|\mathbf{x}|\leq b}|\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})|^{q}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}+Cb^{-n+\alpha}\int_{b-1}^{b}r^{n-1}{\rm d}r

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}}+1+b^{-1+\alpha}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}}+1\big).

Using (
A.5
) and (
A.19
), we have

(A.20)

\displaystyle\int_{|\mathbf{x}|\leq b}\Big|\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})-\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})\Big|^{q}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}+\int_{|\mathbf{x}|\leq b}\Big|\sqrt{\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})}-\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})}\Big|^{2q}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\int_{|\mathbf{x}|\leq b}\Big|\sqrt{\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})}-\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})}\Big|^{2q}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{|\mathbf{x}|\leq b}\Big|\sqrt{\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})}+\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})}\Big|^{2q}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}}+1\big)\Big(\int_{b-1\leq|\mathbf{x}|\leq b}|\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})|^{q}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}+b^{-n+\alpha}\int_{b-1}^{b}r^{n-1}{\rm d}r\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}}+1\big)\Big(\int_{b-2\leq|\mathbf{x}|\leq b+1}\big(|\rho_{0}(\mathbf{x})|^{q}+e^{-r^{2}}\big)\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r+b^{-1+\alpha}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\longrightarrow 0

as
b\rightarrow\infty
, which implies (
A.17
).

For (
A.18
), a direct calculation shows that

\displaystyle\nabla\sqrt{\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})}

\displaystyle=\nabla\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})}\,\big(1-S(2(|\mathbf{x}|-(b-1)))\big)

\displaystyle\quad-2\big(\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})}-\sqrt{b^{-n+\alpha}}\big)\,S^{\prime}(2(|\mathbf{x}|-(b-1)))\frac{\mathbf{x}}{|\mathbf{x}|},

which, together with (
A.7
), yields that

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{b}\Big|\nabla\sqrt{\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})}\Big|^{2}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{b}\Big|\nabla\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})}\Big|^{2}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}+C\varepsilon^{2}\int_{b-1\leq|\mathbf{x}|\leq b}\big(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})+b^{-n+\alpha}\big)\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{1}}+1\big)\varepsilon^{2}.

∎

In general, since
\displaystyle\int_{b^{-1}\leq|\mathbf{x}|\leq b}\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\neq M
, we define

(A.21)

\displaystyle\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x}):=\frac{M}{\int_{b^{-1}\leq|\mathbf{x}|\leq b}\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}}\tilde{\rho}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x}).

Combining (
A.21
) and Lemma
A.5
, we have

Lemma A.6

The smooth function
\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})
defined in (
A.21
) satisfies (
3.12
)
and

(A.22)

\displaystyle\int_{b^{-1}\leq|\mathbf{x}|\leq b}\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}=M\qquad\mbox{for all $\varepsilon\in(0,1]$ and $b>1$},

(A.23)

\displaystyle\int_{|\mathbf{x}|\leq b}\Big(\big|\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})-\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})\big|^{q}+\Big|\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})}-\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})}\Big|^{2q}\Big)\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\rightarrow 0\,\,\mbox{as $b\to\infty$},

(A.24)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\int_{|\mathbf{x}|\leq b}\Big|\nabla_{\mathbf{x}}\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})}\Big|^{2}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\leq C\big(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{1}}+1\big),

where
q\in\{1,\gamma\}
for
\kappa=1

(
gaseous stars
)
, and
q\in\{1,\gamma,\frac{2n}{n+2}\}
for
\kappa=-1

(
plasmas
)
.

We define

(A.25)

\displaystyle\Delta\Phi_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}=\kappa\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}\,\mathbf{1}_{\{b^{-1}\leq|\mathbf{x}|\leq b\}}(\mathbf{x}),

where
{\mathbf{1}}_{\{b^{-1}\leq|\mathbf{x}|\leq b\}}(\mathbf{x})
is the indicator function of set
\{b^{-1}\leq|\mathbf{x}|\leq b\}
.
Using (
A.23
) and by a direct calculation yield

Lemma A.7

\Phi_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}
satisfy

(A.26)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\mathbf{x}}(\Phi_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}-\Phi_{0}^{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}\,\mathbf{1}_{\{b^{-1}\leq|\mathbf{x}|\leq b\}}(\mathbf{x})-\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{n+2}}}\rightarrow 0\,\,\mbox{as $b\rightarrow\infty$}.

Next, we construct the approximate initial data for the velocity.
We denote
\mathbf{1}_{[4b^{-1},b-2]}
to be the indicator
function of
\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\ :\ 4b^{-1}\leq|\mathbf{x}|\leq b-2\}

and define
u_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})
and
\tilde{u}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})
as

(A.27)

\displaystyle u_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x}):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})}}(\frac{m_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}})(\mathbf{x}),

(A.28)

\displaystyle\tilde{u}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\textbf{x}):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big(\frac{m_{0}\mathbf{1}_{[4b^{-1},b-2]}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}}\Big)(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\,J_{b^{-1}}(\textbf{y})\,{\rm d}\textbf{y},

where
\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}
is the function defined in (
A.8
).
Clearly,
\tilde{u}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})
is a spherically symmetric function,

i.e.
,
\tilde{u}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})=\tilde{u}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(r)
.

Lemma A.8

u^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\mathbf{x})
defined in (
A.27
) satisfies

(A.29)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})|u_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})|^{2}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\equiv\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|m_{0}(\mathbf{x})|^{2}}{\rho_{0}(\mathbf{x})}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\qquad\,\,\,\mbox{for any $\varepsilon\in(0,1]$},

(A.30)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0+}\|\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}u^{\varepsilon}_{0}-m_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}=0.

Moreover,
\tilde{u}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})
defined in (
A.28
) is in
C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})

and satisfies

(A.31)

\displaystyle{\rm supp}\,\tilde{u}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}\subset\big\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}~:~2b^{-1}\leq|\mathbf{x}|\leq b-1\big\},

(A.32)

\displaystyle\lim_{b\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})|\tilde{u}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})|^{2}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})|u_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})|^{2}{\rm d}\mathbf{x},

(A.33)

\displaystyle\lim_{b\rightarrow\infty}\|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}\tilde{u}^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}-\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}u^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}=0.

Proof.

(
A.29
) follows directly from (
A.27
).
Using (
A.10
) and (
A.27
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\big|(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}u^{\varepsilon}_{0}-m_{0})(\mathbf{x})\big|\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\big|\big(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}}-\sqrt{\rho_{0}}\big)(\mathbf{x})\big(\frac{m_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}}\big)(\mathbf{x})\big|\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|m_{0}(\mathbf{x})|^{2}}{\rho_{0}(\mathbf{x})}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\big|\big(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}}-\sqrt{\rho_{0}}\big)(\mathbf{x})\big|^{2}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\longrightarrow 0\quad\,\,\mbox{as $\varepsilon\rightarrow 0+$},

which leads to (
A.30
).

From (
A.28
), it is clear that
\tilde{u}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})

and
{\rm supp}\,\tilde{u}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}\subset\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}~:~2b^{-1}\leq|\mathbf{x}|\leq b-1\}
.
For any given small constant
\epsilon>0
, there exists small
\sigma=\sigma(\epsilon)>0
and large
N=N(\epsilon)\gg 1
such that

(A.34)

\displaystyle\int_{B_{2\sigma}(\mathbf{0})\cup\{|\mathbf{x}|\geq N\}}\frac{|m_{0}(\mathbf{x})|^{2}}{\rho_{0}(\mathbf{x})}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\leq\epsilon.

Taking
b>0
large enough so that
\sigma\geq 6b^{-1}
, then it follows from (
A.28
) that

(A.35)

\displaystyle\int_{\sigma\leq|\mathbf{x}|\leq N+2}\Big|\Big(\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}}\tilde{u}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}-\frac{m_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}}\Big)(\mathbf{x})\Big|^{2}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\,\,\mbox{as $b\rightarrow\infty$}.

Since
\sigma\geq 6b^{-1}
, we use (
A.34
) to obtain

(A.36)

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\sigma}(\mathbf{0})\cup\{|\mathbf{x}|\geq N+1\}}\big|\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})}\tilde{u}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})\big|^{2}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{\sigma}(\mathbf{0})\cup\{|\mathbf{x}|\geq N+1\}}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\big(\frac{m_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}}\mathbf{1}_{[4b^{-1},b-2]}\big)(\mathbf{x-y})J_{b^{-1}}(\mathbf{y})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{y}\Big|^{2}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{2\sigma}(\mathbf{0})\cup\{|\mathbf{x}|\geq N\}}\frac{|m_{0}(\mathbf{x})|^{2}}{\rho_{0}(\mathbf{x})}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\leq\epsilon.

It follows from (
A.27
)–(
A.28
) and (
A.35
)–(
A.36
) that

(A.37)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\big|\big(\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}}\tilde{u}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}-\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}}u_{0}^{\varepsilon}\big)(\mathbf{x})\big|^{2}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\big|\big(\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}}\tilde{u}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}-\frac{m_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}}\big)(\mathbf{x})\big|^{2}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\sigma\leq|\mathbf{x}|\leq N+2}\big|\big(\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}}\tilde{u}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}-\frac{m_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}}\big)(\mathbf{x})\big|^{2}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle\quad+C\int_{B_{2\sigma}(\mathbf{0})\cup\{|\mathbf{x}|\geq N\}}\frac{|m_{0}(\mathbf{x})|^{2}}{\rho_{0}(\mathbf{x})}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle\rightarrow 0

as
b\rightarrow\infty
, which yields (
A.32
).

Using (
A.23
), (
A.31
), and (
A.37
), we see that, as
b\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\big|(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}\tilde{u}^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}-\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}u^{\varepsilon}_{0})(\mathbf{x})\big|{\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\mathbf{x})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\big|\big(\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}}\tilde{u}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}-\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}}u_{0}^{\varepsilon}\big)(\mathbf{x})\big|^{2}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{|\mathbf{x}|\leq b}\Big|\big(\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}}-\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}}\big)(\mathbf{x})\,\big(\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}}\tilde{u}^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}\big)(\mathbf{x})\Big|\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\longrightarrow 0,

which implies (
A.33
).
∎

We still need to modify
\tilde{u}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})
so that it satisfies the stress-free boundary condition (
3.4
) at
r=b
.
Let
S(\cdot)
be the function in (
A.15
). Then we define

(A.38)

u_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x}):=\tilde{u}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\,S(4(|\mathbf{x}|-(b-\frac{1}{2})))\,\frac{1}{|\mathbf{x}|^{n-1}}\int_{|\mathbf{x}|}^{b}\frac{p(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(z))}{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(z)}\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z.

It is direct to check that
u_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\textbf{x})\in C^{\infty}([b^{-1},b])
satisfies
the following boundary conditions:

(A.39)

\displaystyle u_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(b^{-1})=0,\qquad\,\,\Big\{p(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b})-\varepsilon\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}\big(u^{\varepsilon,b}_{0r}+\frac{n-1}{r}u^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}\big)\Big\}\Big|_{r=b}=0.

A direct calculation shows from (
A.16
) that

(A.40)

\displaystyle\int_{|\textbf{x}|\leq b}\Big|\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\textbf{x})}u_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\textbf{x})-\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})}\tilde{u}_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})\Big|^{2}{\rm d}\textbf{x}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{a_{0}^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{b-\frac{1}{2}}^{b}\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(r)r^{n-1}\Big|\frac{1}{r^{n-1}}\int_{r}^{b}(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(z))^{\gamma-1}\,z^{n-1}{\rm d}z\Big|^{2}{\rm d}r

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{-2}b^{-2(\gamma-1)(n-\alpha)}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as $b\rightarrow\infty$}.

Therefore, combining (
A.31
), (
A.37
), and (
A.40
), we conclude

Lemma A.9

For fixed
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\lim_{b\rightarrow\infty}\int_{|\mathbf{x}|\leq b}\big|\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})}u_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})\big|^{2}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\big|\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})}u_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})\big|^{2}{\rm d}\mathbf{x},

\displaystyle\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})}u_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})\longrightarrow\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})}u_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})\quad\mbox{in $L^{2}(\{|\mathbf{x}|\leq b\})\,$ as $b\rightarrow\infty$}.

With
\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\mathbf{x})
,
\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}(\mathbf{x})
,
u_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})
,
and
u_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x})
defined respectively in (
A.8
), (
A.21
), (
A.27
), and (
A.38
),
we can construct the approximate initial data

(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b},m_{0}^{\varepsilon,b})(r)=(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b},\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}u_{0}^{\varepsilon,b})(r)
for (
3.1
)–(
3.6
):
For
b\gg 1
, define

(A.41)

\displaystyle(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0},u^{\varepsilon,b}_{0})(r):=(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathbf{x}),u^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}(\mathbf{x}))\mathbf{1}_{[b^{-1},b]}(\mathbf{x}).

Then, collecting all the above estimates, we have the following results.

Lemma A.10

Let
\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0},u^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}\big)(r)
be the functions defined in (
A.41
)
so that
(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0},u^{\varepsilon,b}_{0})
is in
C^{\infty}([b^{-1},b])
and satisfies the boundary condition (
A.39
).
Let
q\in\{1,\gamma\}
for
\kappa=1

(
gaseous stars
)
, and
q\in\{1,\gamma,\frac{2n}{n+2}\}
for
\kappa=-1

(
plasmas
)
.
Then

(i)

For all
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
,

\qquad\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(r)\,\omega_{n}r^{n-1}{\rm d}r=M,\qquad\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|\partial_{r}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}(r)}\big|^{2}\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r\leq C\varepsilon(M+1).

Moreover, as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0+
,

\displaystyle\qquad(E^{\varepsilon}_{0},E_{1}^{\varepsilon})\longrightarrow(E_{0},0),

\displaystyle\qquad(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon},m_{0}^{\varepsilon})(r)\longrightarrow(\rho_{0},m_{0})(r)\quad\mbox{in $L^{q}([0,\infty);r^{n-1}{\rm d}r)\times L^{1}([0,\infty);r^{n-1}{\rm d}r)$},

\displaystyle\qquad\Phi_{0r}^{\varepsilon}\longrightarrow\Phi_{0r}\quad\mbox{in $L^{2}([0,\infty);r^{n-1}{\rm d}r)$},

where
E^{\varepsilon}_{0},E_{1}^{\varepsilon}
, and
E_{0}
are defined in (
2.16
), (
2.17
), and (
2.1
), respectively.
Moreover, there exists a small constant
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that

M<M_{\rm c}^{\varepsilon}(\gamma)\quad\mbox{for all $\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]$ and $\gamma\in(\frac{2n}{n+2},\frac{2(n-1)}{n}]$},

where
M_{\rm c}^{\varepsilon}(\gamma)
is defined in (
3.26
).

(ii)

For
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
and
b>1
,

\qquad\int_{b^{-1}}^{b}\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(r)\,\omega_{n}r^{n-1}{\rm d}r=M,\quad\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{b}\big|\partial_{r}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}(r)}\big|^{2}\,r^{n-1}{\rm d}r\leq C\varepsilon(M+1).

Moreover, for any fixed
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, as
b\rightarrow\infty
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\qquad(E_{0}^{\varepsilon,b},E_{1}^{\varepsilon,b})\longrightarrow(E_{0}^{\varepsilon},E_{1}^{\varepsilon}),\\
&\qquad(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b},m_{0}^{\varepsilon,b})(r)\longrightarrow(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon}_{0})(r)\quad\mbox{\rm in $L^{q}([0,b];r^{n-1}{\rm d}r)\times L^{1}([0,b];r^{n-1}{\rm d}r)$},\\
&\qquad\Phi_{0r}^{\varepsilon,b}\to\Phi^{\varepsilon}_{0r}\quad\mbox{\rm in $L^{2}([0,\infty);r^{n-1}{\rm d}r)$},\end{split}

where
E_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}
and
E_{1}^{\varepsilon,b}
are defined in (
3.7
) and (
3.8
), respectively.
Furthermore, there exists a constant
\mathfrak{B}(\varepsilon)\gg 1
such that

(A.42)

M<M_{\rm c}^{\varepsilon,b}(\gamma)\qquad\mbox{for $\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}],\,\,b\geq\mathfrak{B}(\varepsilon)$, and $\gamma\in(\frac{2n}{n+2},\frac{2(n-1)}{n}]$},

where
M_{\rm c}^{\varepsilon,b}(\gamma)
is defined in (
3.9
).
